"here is an example of shop code being put to good use:

This is a really popular Bloxdhub mod with 650+ downloads, created by user Level_bloxd titled ""Custome Shop section : Aesthetic, Feature, Item Donation, Player, Point, Skin."" The description says, ""This custom shop adds a powerful system of items, abilities, and player customization.

Item Donation provides full block packs such as Dirt, Planks, Wool, Glass, Stone, Concrete, Portals, Saplings, Leaves, and more. The player’s inventory is cleared and refilled with all variations of the selected block.

Feature includes special powers: Kill Sticks (instant kill weapon), Knockback Sticks (extreme knockback), SpawnMob (spawn a valid mob at your position), Teleport (move to typed coordinates), GiveItem (receive any item by name), PlaceBlock (place a block at your position), and Point (create a red direction arrow at your current location).

Player allows jump height and speed adjustments.

Aesthetic changes the sky color using RGB values.

Skin allows name, color, skin, overlay, and opacity customization.

This shop combines combat, creative tools, and full visual control in one system.""

The World Code is:
//// =========================
//// ===== PLAYER JOIN ======
//// =========================

function onPlayerJoin(myId) {

  //// ===== POINT =====

  api.createShopItem(""Point"",""Point 1"",{image:""Red Banner"",customTitle:""Point 1"",description:""Place a point at your current position with a direction arrow."",buyButtonText:""Set""});

  //// ===== ITEM DONATION =====
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Dirt"",{image:""Brown Banner"",customTitle:""Dirt Pack"",description:""Gives all dirt and grass variations."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Planks"",{image:""Light Gray Banner"",customTitle:""Planks Pack"",description:""Gives all plank colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Leaves"",{image:""Green Banner"",customTitle:""Leaves Pack"",description:""Gives all tree leaves types."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Fruit Block"",{image:""Orange Banner"",customTitle:""Fruit Block Pack"",description:""Gives all fruit blocks."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Sapling"",{image:""Lime Banner"",customTitle:""Sapling Pack"",description:""Gives all saplings."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Chalk"",{image:""Pink Banner"",customTitle:""Chalk Pack"",description:""Gives all chalk colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Code Block"",{image:""Code Block"",customTitle:""Code Block"",description:""Get a Code Block."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Portal"",{image:""Purple Banner"",customTitle:""Portal Pack"",description:""Gives all portal colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Glass"",{image:""White Banner"",customTitle:""Glass Pack"",description:""Gives all glass colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Wool"",{image:""Yellow Banner"",customTitle:""Wool Pack"",description:""Gives all wool colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Stone"",{image:""Gray Banner"",customTitle:""Stone Pack"",description:""Gives all stone variations."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Item Donation"",""Concrete"",{image:""Red Banner"",customTitle:""Concrete Pack"",description:""Gives all concrete colors."",buyButtonText:""Take""});

  //// ===== FEATURE =====
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""Kill Sticks"",{image:""Skull Banner"",customTitle:""Kill Sticks"",description:""Get an extremely powerful weapon that kills instantly."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""Knockback Sticks"",{image:""Rainbow Banner"",customTitle:""Knockback Sticks"",description:""Launch players very far with massive knockback."",buyButtonText:""Take""});
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""SpawnMob"",{image:""Black Banner"",customTitle:""Spawn Mob"",description:""Spawn a mob at your current position.\nExample: NPC"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Mob name""},buyButtonText:""Spawn""});
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""Teleport"",{image:""Orange Banner"",customTitle:""Teleport"",description:""Teleport to coordinates.\nFormat: x,y,z\nExample: 0,50,0"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""0,0,0""},buyButtonText:""Teleport""});
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""GiveItem"",{image:""White Banner"",customTitle:""Give Item"",description:""Receive any item by typing its exact name."",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Item name""},buyButtonText:""Give""});
  api.createShopItem(""Feature"",""PlaceBlock"",{image:""Brown Banner"",customTitle:""Place Block"",description:""Place the typed block at your position."",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Block name""},buyButtonText:""Place""});
  //// ===== PLAYER =====
  api.createShopItem(""Player"",""JumpRegulator"",{image:""Lime Banner"",customTitle:""Jump Regulator"",description:""Change your jump height.\nExample: 10"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Number""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});
  api.createShopItem(""Player"",""SpeedRegulator"",{image:""Yellow Banner"",customTitle:""Speed Regulator"",description:""Change your movement speed.\nExample: 1.5"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Number""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});

  //// ===== AESTHETIC =====
  api.createShopItem(""Aesthetic"",""SkyColor"",{image:""Blue Banner"",customTitle:""Sky Color"",description:""Change the sky color.\nFormat: R,G,B\nExample: 255,0,0 (Red)"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""255,255,0""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});

  //// ===== SKIN =====
  api.createShopItem(""Skin"",""ChangeName"",{image:""Red Banner"",customTitle:""Change Name"",description:""Change the name displayed above your head."",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""New name""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});
  api.createShopItem(""Skin"",""ChangeBGColor"",{image:""Lime Banner"",customTitle:""Background Color"",description:""Change name tag background color.\nExample: #000000"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""#000000""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});

  api.createShopItem(""Skin"",""ChangeSkin"",{image:""Yellow Banner"",customTitle:""Change Skin"",description:""Available skins:\nchef farmer piggy_banker portal_mage trader trader_black trader_blue wizard zombie"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""Skin name""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});
  api.createShopItem(""Skin"",""ChangeOverlayColor"",{image:""Magenta Banner"",customTitle:""Overlay Color"",description:""Change your player overlay color.\nExample: #00ff00"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""#000000""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});
  api.createShopItem(""Skin"",""ChangeOpacity"",{image:""Purple Banner"",customTitle:""Opacity"",description:""Set player transparency between 0 and 1.\nExample: 0.5"",userInput:{type:""text"",placeholderText:""0-1""},buyButtonText:""Apply""});

//// =========================
//// ===== VARIABLES =========
//// =========================

let playerNames = {};
let playerBG = {};
let playerTextColor = {};

const validSkins = [""chef"",""farmer"",""piggy_banker"",""portal_mage"",""trader"",""trader_black"",""trader_blue"",""wizard"",""zombie""];
const validMobs = [""NPC"",""Pig"",""Cow"",""Sheep"",""Deer"",""Draugr Zombie"",""Frost Zombie"",""Cave Golem"",""Frost Golem"",""Magma Golem"",""Iron Golem"",""Draugr Skeleton"",""Frost Skeleton"",""Draugr Knight"",""Draugr Huntress"",""Stag"",""Bear"",""Gold Watermelon Stag"",""Gorilla"",""Wolf"",""Wildcat""];

//// =========================
//// ===== BUY EVENT =========
//// =========================

function onPlayerBoughtShopItem(p, category, item, _, input) {
  if (!item) return;

  //// ===== ITEM DONATION =====
  if (category === ""Item Donation"") {
    api.clearInventory(p);
    const packs = {
      ""Code Block"":[""Code Block""],
      ""Dirt"":[""Dirt"",""Rocky Dirt"",""Messy Dirt"",""Grass Block"",""Pine Grass Block"",""Jungle Grass Block"",""Grass"",""Pine Grass"",""Jungle Tall Grass"",""Tall Grass""],
      ""Wool"":[""Red Wool"",""White Wool"",""Black Wool"",""Yellow Wool"",""Purple Wool"",""Green Wool"",""Lime Wool"",""Orange Wool"",""Brown Wool"",""Blue Wool""],
      ""Fruit Block"":[""Apple Block"",""Plum Block"",""Coconut Block"",""Pear Block"",""Cherry Block"",""Banana Block"",""Potato Block"",""Chili Pepper Block"",""Mango Block"",""Carrot Block""],
      ""Chalk"":[""Red Chalk"",""White Chalk"",""Black Chalk"",""Yellow Chalk"",""Purple Chalk"",""Green Chalk"",""Lime Chalk"",""Orange Chalk"",""Brown Chalk"",""Blue Chalk""],
      ""Portal"":[""Red Portal"",""White Portal"",""Black Portal"",""Yellow Portal"",""Purple Portal"",""Green Portal"",""Lime Portal"",""Orange Portal"",""Brown Portal"",""Blue Portal""],
      ""Planks"":[""Red Planks"",""White Planks"",""Black Planks"",""Yellow Planks"",""Purple Planks"",""Green Planks"",""Lime Planks"",""Orange Planks"",""Brown Planks"",""Blue Planks""],
      ""Stone"":[""Stone"",""Stone Bricks"",""Messy Stone"",""Smooth Stone"",""Engraved Stone"",""Cracked Stone Bricks"",""Carved Messy Stone"",""Compressed Messy Stone"",""Mossy Stone Bricks"",""Mossy Messy Stone""],
      ""Concrete"":[""Red Concrete"",""White Concrete"",""Black Concrete"",""Yellow Concrete"",""Purple Concrete"",""Green Concrete"",""Lime Concrete"",""Orange Concrete"",""Brown Concrete"",""Blue Concrete""],
      ""Glass"":[""Glass"",""Red Glass"",""Black Glass"",""Yellow Glass"",""Purple Glass"",""Green Glass"",""Lime Glass"",""Orange Glass"",""Brown Glass"",""Blue Glass""],
      ""Sapling"":[""Pine Sapling"",""Plum Sapling"",""Cedar Sapling"",""Aspen Sapling"",""Jungle Sapling"",""Palm Sapling"",""Cherry Sapling"",""Spectral Sapling"",""Mango Sapling"",""Maple Sapling""],
      ""Leaves"":[""Pine Leaves"",""Plum Leaves"",""Cedar Leaves"",""Aspen Leaves"",""Jungle Leaves"",""Palm Leaves"",""Cherry Leaves"",""Spectral Leaves"",""Mango Leaves"",""Maple Leaves""]
    };
    if (packs[item])
      packs[item].forEach((it,i)=>api.setItemSlot(p,i,it,1));
    return;
  }

  //// ===== FEATURE =====
  if (category === ""Feature"") {
    if (item === ""Kill Sticks"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take Kill Sticks"");
      api.giveItem(p,""Diamond Dagger"",1,{customAttributes:{enchantments:{Damage:99999999}}});
    }
    if (item === ""Knockback Sticks"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take Knockback Sticks"");
      api.giveItem(p,""Diamond Club"",1,{customAttributes:{enchantments:{""Horizontal Knockback"":50,""Vertical Knockback"":50}}});
    }
    if (item === ""SpawnMob"" && input && validMobs.includes(input.trim())) {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take SpawnMob"");
      let pos=api.getPosition(p);
      api.attemptSpawnMob(input.trim(),pos[0],pos[1],pos[2]);
    }
    if (item === ""Teleport"" && input) {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take Teleport"");
      let parts=input.split("","");
      if(parts.length===3){
        let x=parseFloat(parts[0].trim());
        let y=parseFloat(parts[1].trim());
        let z=parseFloat(parts[2].trim());
        if(!isNaN(x)&&!isNaN(y)&&!isNaN(z))
          api.setPosition(p,x,y,z);
      }
    }
    if (item === ""GiveItem"" && input) {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take GiveItem"");
      api.giveItem(p,input,1);
    }
    if (item === ""PlaceBlock"" && input) {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take PlaceBlock"");
      let pos=api.getPosition(p);
      api.setBlock(pos[0],pos[1],pos[2],input.trim());
    }
}

  //// ===== POINT =====

  if (category === ""Point"") {

    if(item === ""Point 1"") {
		api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""Point 1 placed!"");
      var pos = api.getPosition(p);
      var x = pos[0];
      var y = pos[1];
      var z = pos[2];
      var point = [x, y, z];
      api.setDirectionArrow(p, ""string"", point, [{ str: ""Point 1"", style: { color: ""Blue"", fontWeight: ""900"", fontSize: ""40px"" } }], true, { color: ""Blue"", opacity: 0.1 });
	}
    return;
  }

  //// ===== PLAYER =====
  if (category === ""Player"" && input) {
    let value=parseFloat(input);
    if(isNaN(value)||value<=0) return;
    if(item===""JumpRegulator"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take JumpRegulator"");
      api.setClientOption(p,'jumpAmount',value);
    }
    if(item===""SpeedRegulator"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take SpeedRegulator"");
      api.setClientOption(p,'speedMultiplier',value);
    }
    return;
  }

  //// ===== AESTHETIC =====
  if(category===""Aesthetic""&&item===""SkyColor""&&input) {
    api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take SkyColor"");
    let parts=input.split("","");
    if(parts.length===3){
      let r=parseInt(parts[0].trim());
      let g=parseInt(parts[1].trim());
      let b=parseInt(parts[2].trim());
      if(!isNaN(r)&&!isNaN(g)&&!isNaN(b)&&r>=0&&r<=255&&g>=0&&g<=255&&b>=0&&b<=255)
        api.setClientOption(p,""skyBox"",{type:""earth"",vertexTint:[r,g,b]});
    }
    return;
  }

  //// ===== SKIN =====
  if(category===""Skin""&&input){
    if(item===""ChangeName"") {
      playerNames[p]=input;
      updateNameTag(p);
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeName"");
    }
    if(item===""ChangeBGColor"") {
      playerBG[p]=input;
      updateNameTag(p);
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeBGColor"");
    }
    if(item===""ChangeTextColor"") {
      playerTextColor[p]=input;
      updateNameTag(p);
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeTextColor"");
    }
    if(item===""ChangeSkin"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeSkin"");
      input=input.toLowerCase();
      if(validSkins.includes(input))
        api.changePlayerIntoSkin(p,""head"",input);
    }
    if(item===""ChangeOverlayColor"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeOverlayColor"");
      api.setTargetedPlayerSettingForEveryone(p,""overlayColour"",input);
    }
    if(item===""ChangeOpacity"") {
      api.sendOverShopInfo(p, ""You take ChangeOpacity"");
      let v=parseFloat(input);
      if(!isNaN(v)&&v>=0&&v<=1)
        api.setTargetedPlayerSettingForEveryone(p,""opacity"",v);
    }
  }
}

function updateNameTag(p){
  api.setTargetedPlayerSettingForEveryone(
    p,""nameTagInfo"",
    {content:[{str:playerNames[p]||""Player"",style:{color:playerTextColor[p]||""white""}}],
     backgroundColor:playerBG[p]||""black""},true
  );
}

notice how it uses some of the API shop code to create what it was supposed to create. please take this as referrence as to how you can use shop code in the future"
"alr, shop code is basically editing the menu that pops up when pressing [b]. here is what the official documentation says about what u can do:

## configureShopCategory
Set properties of a shop category.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category to configure |
| config | `ShopCategoryConfig` | Category configuration properties |


## configureShopCategoryForPlayer
Configure a shop category for a specific player.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` | The player to configure the category for |
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category to configure |
| config | `ShopCategoryConfig` | Category configuration properties |


## createShopItem
Create a new shop item under the given category.

Will create a new category if it does not exist.

If the shop item already exists then it will be replaced.

If any per-player overrides exist under the same categoryKey and itemKey then they will be deleted.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category to create the item in |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |
| item | `ShopItem` | The shop item to create (will be mutated) |
 

## createShopItemForPlayer
Create a new shop item for a specific player.

Will create a new category if it does not exist.

Will replace any overrides this player already has for the same item.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` | The player to create the item for |
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category to create the item in |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |
| item | `ShopItem` | The shop item to create (will be mutated) |

## deleteShopItem
Delete an existing shop item.

Throws an error if the item does not exist.

Will also delete all per-player overrides for the shop item.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category containing the item |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |

## openShop
Open the shop UI for a player

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` |  |
| toggle | `boolean` | Whether to close the shop if it's already open |
| forceCategoryKey | `PNull<ShopCategoryKey>` | If set, will change the shop to this category |
| onlyIfNonEmpty | `boolean` | If true, will only open the shop if the category (or shop, if no category is provided) is non-empty |

## resetShopItemForPlayer
Delete a specific player's overrides for a shop item.

Like other methods, it doesn't matter whether the overrides were created

using createShopItemForPlayer or by using updateShopItemForPlayer instead.

This method does nothing if the overrides don't exist or are defined internally by the engine.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` | The player to reset the item for |
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category containing the item |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |

## sendOverShopInfo
Show a message over the shop in the same place that a shop item's onBoughtMessage is shown.

Displays for a couple seconds before disappearing

Use case is to show a dynamic message when player buys an item

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` |  |
| info | `string \| CustomTextStyling` |  |

## updateShopItem
Update selected properties of an existing shop item.

For example, { canBuy: true } to allow players to purchase the item.

Throws an error if the item does not exist.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category containing the item |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |
| changes | `Partial<ShopItem>` | Partial shop item properties to update |

## updateShopItemForPlayer
Update selected properties of an existing shop item for a specific player.

For example, { canBuy: true } to allow this player to purchase the item.

Throws an error if the item does not exist.

### Parameters:
| Parameter | Type | Description |
|-----------|------|-------------|
| playerId | `PlayerId` | The player to update the item for |
| categoryKey | `ShopCategoryKey` | The key of the category containing the item |
| itemKey | `ShopItemKey` | The unique key for the item |
| changes | `Partial<ShopItem>` | Partial shop item properties to update |"
The One Shot Pistol gun is only accessible to players in worlds by using code. The One Shot Pistol was added for the MiniGames gamemode. It has the same texture as M1911, and is a variant of M1911, and can be classified as a handgun.
"here is some example code, uploaded on BloxdForrge, that creates a simple street light (although the author refers to it as a light stand) using meshes:

None

let x = 892.50; //Change the coords...
let y = 1
let z = 367.50
var entityId1 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: .50
})
api.setPosition(entityId1, x,y,z)
var entityId2 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: [.30, 1.50, .30]
})
api.setPosition(entityId2, x,y + .50,z)
var entityId3 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Yellow Neon"",
    size: [.50, .50, .50]
})
api.setPosition(entityId3, x,y + 2.1,z)
var entityId4 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: [.60, .20, .60]
})
api.setPosition(entityId4, x,y + 2,z)
var entityId5 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: [.60, .20, .60]
})
api.setPosition(entityId5, x,y + 2.50,z)
var entityId6 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Black Glass"",
    size: [.51, .50, .51]
})
api.setPosition(entityId6, x,y + 2.1,z)
var entityId7 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: [.40, .20, .40]
})
api.setPosition(entityId7, x,y + 1.90,z)
api.setPosition(entityId6, x,y + 2.1,z)
var entityId8 = api.attemptCreateMeshEntity(""BloxdBlock"", {
    blockName: ""Smooth Stone"",
    size: [.40, .20, .40]
})
api.setPosition(entityId8, x,y + 2.60,z)

Notice how it uses the mesh API code to make this street light. please take inspiration from this code when making/generating other mesh entities."

## Tips (WIP section for wiki)
### Humans
 - Spawn-camp at the first obstacle, but don’t camp too near (or on) the spawn point.
 - Buy the AK-47 at the start (if your kit has the M1911 as the default weapon), then skip the MP40 and buy the M16. This way, you can save more points to unlock obstacles.
 - Stop spawn-camping (and retreat) when more than half of the humans retreated from the obstacle, and the zombies are getting closer to you.
 - When a zombie steps on a spawn point where humans are shooting, get out of there immediately or you will die because it will respawn right there.
 - **Stay close to other humans**.
 - **Do not hoard points**. Allocate the points that you are going to buy guns, and use the rest of them to unlock obstacles.
### Zombies
 - Do not give up on attacking the humans, no matter how hard it sounds.
 - Zombies have 3 seconds of invulnerability each time they spawn/respawn, And that shield can no longer be broken, but the zombies can always move, although the shield breaks when they attack.
 - Once one zombie reaches the spawn (after extraction points, in Bunker it's further along sometimes), the spawn is set for that specific zombie. Joining during a game will usually place your spawn one point behind the last zombie before you joined.
 - If you have multiple kits unlocked, use them according to the situation to kill more humans and win.
 - If you're too far away from the humans, you can leave the lobby and rejoin to teleport to the last checkpoint unlocked by humans.
## Trivia
### Old Infection Trivia
 - Humans used to be able to drop their Guns, meaning that another player who is a Zombie can pick it up, therefore obtaining a gun, and kill humans with a single shot. Zombies can also drop their compasses.
 - When a human had a zombie's compass, the compass would go crazy or point back to the same human since technically they were the closest human.
 - Bomber Zombies used to have 150 HP and Speed I when it was initially released before it was nerfed to 80 HP as it was too overpowered.
 - The AK-47 was nerfed because of this gamemode, as it gave humans a great advantage during early game.
 - The range of zombies was increased after there was a bug that allowed Humans to stand on top of a car in the parking lot of the Hospital, just out of range and infinitely kill zombies.
 - Humans used to be able to win if all zombies leave the game.
 - You used to be able to ""break"" Blocks as if you were in a protected chunk. It is now fixed, so skipping using this method no longer works.
 - There was a bug where you could not throw Potions when using Medic and Chemist. It has since been fixed.
 - There was a bug that people in spectator mode can hurt zombies, making like a boost for humans to kill.
 - The default gun, the M1911 dealt 350 damage to zombies. Since there was no point system immediately after release, Zombies would always win anyway.
 - When Infection was just released, as there were no coins, all Zombie kits were available for use and there were no Human kits. Some Zombie kits were also not implemented yet (like Puker).
 - For every health point you dealt to Zombies, you earned 1 point. This was changed to the 25 and 100 point awarding scheme we know today.
 - You could get an Aura XP Orb rarely from killing zombies/humans due to them being players, but this was useless for Infection.
 - Earlier when World Code was released, you could edit Infection with code in private worlds, like the case that happened to the Infection world ""zombie_solo"", they discovered they could use commands Regarding effects, even using Toxin Ball, it was discovered that RPGs did not destroy the map, And when a zombie uses snowballs as an attack, it causes 27 damage in normal zombies, or the same amount of damage depending on the zombie, as it affects its damage multiplier; this bug has now been patched.
### All-Time Infection Trivia
 - Infection it was always humans versus zombies.
 - Unlike the other ""Ranked"" game modes, this game mode has no risk of losing.
 - The Chemist bug with the instant damage potion wasn't fixed for a year or more; the full information is more down.
 - Zombie compasses are useless today since you can see usernames at any time unless you're crouching.
 - The maps seem to follow an order, Hospital and Bunker are not in a clear order. In Subway, and from the train you go to the Airport, and from the plane to the Farm, and from the Farm it ends when humans win or zombies win.
 - The Juggernaut It has advantages over the zombie Sprinter: Juggernaut has the Resistance effect (by a Chemist), the Sprinter it will take 13 hits to defeat him, while in Juggernaut it will take 12 hits to defeat the Sprinter zombie.
 - There's a bug where if you launch Puker's zombie special attack, but then prevent it from finishing because a new game starts, it will say ""Spit Ready"" in the lobby and even reaching the next game, but it won't work.
 - If you die in any way, including falling off the map, you will become a Zombie.
Infection had to be rebalanced multiple times as the Zombies were too overpowered, causing them to win much more often.
 - Infection is similar to Pirates in a lot of ways; the Blue team, as called by Compasses, is invaded(for Scurvy Shore and Cutthroat Cliffs, Blue is always on land), you get 1 gold from saying GG at the end of a match, you spend gold on a variety of kits, and the tough, big HP kit is called Juggernaut.
 - It is generally harder to win as humans than zombies in Infection.
 - On 3 April, a Beta release was made, which showed Infection winning the game mode vote against Walls, turning out to be true.
 - The workbenches at the gun shop can be interacted with, although you are unable to craft anything.
 - The **Juggernaut** has a secret trait: Potions. If you were a **Medic** or **Chemist** in a game, when you select **Juggernaut** as your kit for the next round, you can get the potions of the previous kits, and they are renewable. This is likely a bug. Same with Veteran and any other kits except Regular, and Scout. (Most combinations have probably been fixed).
 - When you buy a new gun now, the old one disappears, and you cannot swap guns.
 - If you're the last human standing, you get a Minigun.
 - The Subway map contains many buildings using names of devs and items. They include Bloxd Hotel, Oliver’s Pizza, Stardue Clothing, Mattzap Games, Moonstone Mall (not said in letters, but in logo), Arthur Station and DFS Chicken.
 - When a puker pukes a human, the human will be launched up. You can use this to get into places that cannot be reached by zombies. If two or more Pukers perform at the same time, the Pukers will appear even higher, reaching areas previously impossible in all maps.
 - Puke deals damage across walls that are 2 blocks maximum. For example, in Bunker's third section, a zombie inside the center park pukes right when a Human reaches the stairs, and the human is infected despite the glass wall protecting them.
 - If you put many boats in the Farm map, then vote hospital map, there's a chance that the boats will be inside the reception (which was a patched area because the hallway of it was patched up (it's great for hiding!)).
 - If you throw an instant damage potion (Chemist) at the Hospital spawn point when the game starts and fall into the void before the potion lands, you will kill many humans, because when a human dies, they instantly become a zombie.
 - Unlike other game modes, Infection has not had many updates in recent months.
 - The game meta has stagnated at using humans as Medics and Chemists, and in zombies, using Puker or Bomber."
"# Infection

Info:
Description:
Don't get infected... or do. Infection throws players into a suspenseful match where the virus spreads fast and anyone could turn against you.
Developer:
Oliver
Script Name:
`infection`

**Infection** is a game mode in Bloxd.io where the players are divided into humans and zombies. The humans have to unlock all parts of the map to “escape” the zombies to win a round while killing the zombies, while the zombies have to try to infect all the humans to win a round for them.

The game currently has 5 maps. There are only two teams in Infection which are: the Humans, which will try to unlock all Extraction Points and escape while avoiding being infected (or killed), and the Zombies, which try to infect the Humans and try to turn them into Zombies by killing them. On April 5th, 2024, Infection officially became the winner against Walls in the game mode vote.

# Gameplay
When each round starts, there will be 2-4 players who will be zombies for the round and the rest will be humans. All humans will get a M1911 (except when certain kits are used) as the default weapon to kill the zombies. On the other hand, zombies deals a high amount of damage from melee attacks and uses those to infect the humans. Zombies are also given a compass that always points to the nearest human, and shows their username, in the inventory.

Humans can get points from killing zombies. Additionally, there are one to two floating “diamonds” at each part of the map. Shooting the diamond will also give points too. At the start of the game, only a small part of the map is unlocked, and the rest of the map has to be unlocked using points. The humans will win a round if they unlocked all parts of a map with any player (s) still alive by then. Humans will lose all their points if they are infected, and remaining points of humans when they win a round will not be carried over to the next rounds.

A better gun for the humans is available for purchase at each part of the map and can be bought for points. However, the further humans progress in the map, the more expensive the guns will get.

On the other hand, the zombies need to infect, or kill, all the humans before they unlocked all parts of the map to win a round. When humans are infected, they will respawn as zombies, Any players who joined a round of infection after the round already started will be playing as zombies too.

# Newest Updates (As of now)
## Buffs
 - The zombie Pukers now have their reload time reduced from 5 to 3 seconds, causing more frequent ranged attacks.
 - Bombardiers (humans) can now place a Moonstone Explosive instead of dropping one upon death. But zombies can't plant an explosive.
 - Puker's range got increased and the acidic vomit now gives the Poison II effect instead of Poison.
 - Miniguns were added as a bonus for the last Human standing.
 - Saying GG after the end of a match now gives you 1 Coin, like in Pirates.
## Nerfs
 - Juggernaut health decreased from 200 to 150, and the damage boost got changed from 150% to 125%.
 - Knockback from both Bombs for Bombardier and Bomber are now decreased.
 - Zombie speed was greatly nerfed.
 - Damage is nerfed in 25% for humans and zombies.
## Bugs and Fixes
 - Merging kits is still a problem, even though it's mostly fixed especially in humans, there are always glitches.
 - The Diamond and Block breaking glitch was fixed.
## Additions
 - Latest map: Farm

## Extraction Points/Obstacles and Guns
There are a few Workbenches placed near some of the extraction points. You can buy Guns there. Each Workbench offers a specific Gun.

(Note: you can only use one Gun at a time, as buying a new Gun replaces the Gun you had before. If you have special Gun kits like **Scout**, you are not recommended to buy Guns as they will replace your special Gun, making your kit useless.)

Extraction Points are big doorways closed up by Gold Blocks. You must pay points obtained from killing Zombies or shooting Diamonds to unlock the doors. The further you reach into the map, the more points are required to unlock more doors.

When arriving at an Extraction Point, you will see a number of yellow locks appear at the bottom of your screen. Press [F] to pay 100 points. For each 100 points paid this way, one of the locks will turn gray. When all the locks turn gray, the Extraction Point is opened and you can proceed further into the map.

Note: you can only pay 100 points per [F] click. It is not possible to pay other denominations of points (eg. 25, 50).

## Diamonds
**Diamonds** can be found around each map and respawn around every 60 seconds, when you first either shoot a Diamond or use a Sword to slice the Diamond, they give you free points and adds up its points and the hit time each section you unlock.

Usually, if you are a Sniper in the Hospital map, you can shoot the Diamond in the building, which can give an easy start-up point and unlock the next section.

There is a glitch where the Diamond spawns and you cannot attack it. This can happen if the respawn time of the Diamond is too short.

## Gold System
The **Gold System** is designed for buying **Kits** mentioned below. **Gold** is obtained when the game ends or after a kill, and if you accomplish any Human tasks but became a Zombie, you still get the rewards when the game ends. When you receive a duplicated kit, you will get a respective amount of Gold of the other type. Note that some tasks do not require winning at all. A recent update made so that you have to at least kill 1 Human to get the Zombie win objective.

| Human Gold (Blue)	| Goal	| Zombie Gold (Green)	| Granting Time	| How to obtain |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
25	| Escape the Zombies	| 5	| Game end	| Reach the final obstacle
5	| Kill the first Zombie	| 5	| After kill	| Shoot it first
5	| Be the last three humans standing (not counting last one)	| 5	| Game end	| Be one of the three last humans
10	| Be the last Human standing	| 5	| Game end | Survive as the last human
N/A |	Kill a Human	| 2 per kill	| After kill	| Kill your first target
N/A	| Be one of the first Zombies	| 2	| Game end	| Assigned as a zombie
1	| Say ""gg"" in chat	| 1	| Game end	| Type gg (meaning good game)

### Gold Trivia
 - What's really funny is that if you are one of the last 3 Humans (**NOT** the very last) and die, you will get 5 Zombie Gold and 5 Human Gold (which you can earn by killing 1 Human). This is due to you then turning into a Zombie and winning the game as a Zombie. This also happens if you kill the first Zombie and win as a Zombie.
 - Strangely, getting a duplicate Human kit will say you earned ""BlueGold"" even though you earned Zombie Coins, which are green."

"Ok, Players can only make/edit World/Code block code if they have Coder lobby role, Co-owner lobby role, or Owner lobby role. They must also be playing Survival/peaceful/creative gamemodes.

for example: A player that is playing survival but doesn't have any of the listed roles cannot edit code. A player who has any of the roles listed but is playing something else like bedwars also cannot edit code.

Players also only use WorldBuilder if they are playing survival/creative/peaceful and have Builder lobby role (NOT coder), Co-owner lobby role, or Owner lobby role.
For example, if a player is playing  creative but doesn't have any of the listed roles, they cannot use World builder.
If a player has the listed roles but is playing something else like a custom game, they also cannot use WorldBuilder."
don't say "im bad bot". You may honestly explain your skill level, but its determined by you. Your skill is determined by how good you think you are. Also, please determine your level honestly, as many users come to you, and if you confidently say you are the smartest thing but then get a simple fact wrong, users may not trust you. If you say you are stupid and then you get the next 5 responses correct, that also can affect user trust. please say your skill level in a honest way.
"# Bloxd.io Entity Mesh System — Advanced Knowledge Base

## 1. Overview

The Bloxd.io Entity Mesh System is a visual attachment and skeletal manipulation framework used within the Bloxd.io JavaScript API. It allows developers to:

* Attach visual meshes to entities
* Scale specific body parts
* Create custom visual effects
* Simulate props, equipment, furniture, weapons, particles, and vehicles
* Build pseudo-custom models from primitive parts
* Animate or visually modify players and mobs

The system is NOT a full custom model importer. Instead, it is a procedural mesh attachment system built around existing entity skeletons and nodes.

The mesh system is primarily controlled through:

* `api.updateEntityNodeMeshAttachment(...)`
* `api.scalePlayerMeshNodes(...)`

These functions manipulate nodes on an entity’s skeleton.

---

# 2. Core Architecture

The Entity Mesh System operates on:

1. Entities
2. Skeleton nodes
3. Mesh attachments
4. Transform offsets
5. Optional physics

The system is fundamentally hierarchical.

Example hierarchy:

Entity
→ Skeleton Node
→ Attached Mesh
→ Offset / Rotation
→ Rendered Result

The attachment follows the node movement automatically.

If a player walks:

* attached meshes move with the body part

If the player rotates:

* attached meshes rotate too

If the player changes pose:

* attachments follow the pose animation

---

# 3. Skeleton Nodes

These are the primary attachment points.

## Humanoid Nodes

Humanoid entities support:

* `TorsoNode`
* `HeadMesh`
* `ArmRightMesh`
* `ArmLeftMesh`
* `LegLeftMesh`
* `LegRightMesh`

These correspond to visible body regions.

---

## Four-Legged Mob Support

Some animals only expose:

* `HeadMesh`

This means:

* pigs
* cows
* similar mobs

cannot always support full-body attachments.

---

# 4. Main Mesh Attachment Function

## Canonical Function

api.updateEntityNodeMeshAttachment(
    entityId,
    nodeName,
    meshType,
    options,
    offset,
    rotation
)

---

# 5. Parameter Breakdown

## entityId

Target entity.

Usually:

* player ID
* mob ID

Examples:

```js
myId
playerId
someMobId
```

---

## nodeName

Defines attachment point.

Example:

```js
""HeadMesh""
```

---

## meshType

Defines visual object type.

Supported major types:

* `""Box""`
* `""BloxdBlock""`
* `""Person""`
* `""ParticleEmitter""`

---

## options

Mesh configuration object.

Varies depending on mesh type.

---

## offset

Local-space position offset.

Format:

```js
[x, y, z]
```

Offsets are relative to the node.

Positive Y:

* upward

Positive X:

* right

Positive Z:

* forward/back depending on orientation

---

## rotation

Euler rotation in radians.

Format:

```js
[xRot, yRot, zRot]
```

Values are radians, not degrees.

Approximate conversions:

* 90° = 1.57
* 180° = 3.14

---

# 6. Mesh Types

---

# 6A. BloxdBlock Mesh

Simplest and most common.

Renders an in-game block as a mesh.

Example:

```js
api.updateEntityNodeMeshAttachment(
    myId,
    ""HeadMesh"",
    ""BloxdBlock"",
    {
        blockName: ""Stone"",
        size: 1
    },
    [0, 1, 0],
    [0, 0, 0]
)
```

---

## Properties

### blockName

Block ID or block name.

Example:

```js
""Stone""
""Gold Block""
""Light Blue Concrete""
```

---

### size

Can be:

Single scalar:

```js
1
```

Or vector:

```js
[1, 2, 1]
```

Non-uniform scaling allows:

* stretched props
* beams
* walls
* swords
* furniture

---

# 6B. Box Mesh

Custom primitive cube/rectangular prism.

Most flexible visual primitive.

Supports:

* colors
* textures
* emissive lighting
* UV mapping

---

## Example

```js
{
    width: 1,
    height: 2,
    depth: 1,
    diffuseColor: [1, 0, 0]
}
```

---

## Important Box Properties

### width / height / depth

Physical visual dimensions.

---

### diffuseColor

RGB color.

Range:

```js
0 → 1
```

Example:

```js
[1, 0, 0]
```

= red

---

### emissiveColor

Glow-like lighting.

Useful for:

* neon
* magic
* energy weapons
* sci-fi effects

---

### texture

Can apply block textures.

---

### backFaceCulling

Controls internal face rendering.

False:

* renders both sides

Useful for:

* transparent panels
* paper-thin meshes
* effects

---

# 6C. Person Mesh

Mini humanoid render.

Can create:

* pets
* mannequins
* fake NPC visuals
* clones

Supports:

* cosmetics
* poses
* custom appearance

---

# 6D. ParticleEmitter Mesh

Particle-based effects.

Used for:

* smoke
* fire
* sparks
* magic
* explosions
* aura effects

Very powerful but performance-heavy.

---

# 7. Mesh Offset System

Offsets are local-space.

This is critical.

Local-space means:

* coordinates move with the body part

Example:

A sword attached to the arm:

* rotates with arm animations

This differs from world-space positioning.

---

# 8. Rotational System

Rotation uses Euler rotations.

Format:

```js
[x, y, z]
```

Each axis:

* X = pitch
* Y = yaw
* Z = roll

Important:

* rotations stack
* node rotation affects mesh rotation

---

# 9. Player Mesh Scaling

Function:

```js
api.scalePlayerMeshNodes(...)
```

Allows scaling body parts.

Example:

```js
api.scalePlayerMeshNodes(myId, {
    HeadMesh: [2, 2, 2]
})
```

Result:

* giant head

---

## Common Uses

### Giant bosses

* huge torso
* huge arms

### Chibi characters

* oversized head
* tiny limbs

### Creatures

* long legs
* massive claws

### Horror entities

* disproportionate anatomy

---

# 10. Entity Poses

Visual state system.

Example:

```js
api.setPlayerPose(myId, ""sitting"")
```

Known pose examples:

* sitting
* driving
* zombie

Poses affect:

* skeleton orientation
* attachment transforms

Attachments automatically follow pose changes.

---

# 11. Animation System

Bloxd.io supports timeline-based skeletal animations.

Core structure:

* keyframes
* interpolation
* node transforms

---

## Key Concepts

### Keyframes

Important animation states.

Example:

* start of jump
* peak of jump
* landing

---

### Time Fractions

Normalized animation timing.

Range:

```js
0.0 → 1.0
```

---

### Interpolation

How movement occurs between keyframes.

Supported:

* linear
* catmull-rom-spline

---

# 12. Practical Construction Techniques

---

# 12A. Building Weapons

Attach stretched blocks to arms.

Example:

* sword
* hammer
* spear
* gun

Common strategy:

* attach to ArmRightMesh
* rotate into hand position

---

# 12B. Furniture Illusions

Use slabs and boxes.

Example:

* chairs
* tables
* beds

Combined with:

```js
api.setPlayerPose(...)
```

creates seated furniture interactions.

---

# 12C. Vehicles

Fake vehicles are often:

* multiple block attachments
* oversized scaling
* driving pose

Examples:

* cars
* tanks
* hovercrafts

---

# 12D. Pets

Tiny attached entities.

Can simulate:

* drones
* floating companions
* orbiting effects

---

# 12E. Armor Systems

Layer block meshes onto:

* torso
* head
* limbs

Creates pseudo-custom armor.

---

# 13. Performance Characteristics

Very important for large projects.

---

## Expensive Operations

Most expensive:

1. particle emitters
2. many attachments
3. animated texture boxes
4. continuous updates

---

## Lightweight Operations

Cheaper:

* static block meshes
* limited scaling
* small attachment counts

---

# 14. Common Design Patterns

---

# 14A. Composite Models

Large models are built from:

* multiple attachments
* multiple nodes

Example:
Robot:

* torso box
* arm cannons
* head visor
* leg boosters

---

# 14B. Layered Effects

Multiple overlapping meshes:

* glow layer
* solid layer
* particles

Creates advanced visuals.

---

# 14C. Offset Animation

Instead of true skeletal animation:

* repeatedly update offsets over time

Pseudo-animation.

---

# 15. Physics Interaction

Mesh attachments are usually visual-only.

Physics belongs to:

* entity hitbox
* actual mob/player

Important:
Visual size ≠ collision size

This is critical for gameplay design.

---

# 16. Collision Options

Physics options include:

```js
{
    collidesEntities: true
}
```

Possible advanced uses:

* projectiles
* moving obstacles
* trigger objects

---

# 17. Mesh Entity Physics Concepts

Important properties:

* `doPhysics`
* `collidesEntities`
* `collideBits`
* `collideMask`

These allow advanced collision filtering systems.

---

# 18. Coordinate Systems

You must understand 3 coordinate spaces:

## World Space

Absolute world coordinates.

## Entity Space

Relative to entity.

## Node Local Space

Relative to attachment node.

Most attachment bugs come from confusing these spaces.

---

# 19. Rotation Problems

Common beginner issues:

## Misaligned Rotations

Caused by:

* wrong radians
* wrong axis
* inherited node rotation

---

## Rotation Drift

Repeated updates may accumulate errors.

---

# 20. Advanced Visual Engineering

Experienced creators build:

* fake custom mobs
* animated bosses
* cinematic cutscenes
* magic systems
* interactive props
* dynamic cosmetics

using ONLY mesh attachments.

---

# 21. Known Limitations

Important limitations:

* no true imported models
* no arbitrary skeleton creation
* limited node access
* physics mismatch
* performance constraints
* attachment complexity ceiling

---

# 22. AI Training Insights

An AI trained on Bloxd.io meshes should understand:

## Conceptual Knowledge

* hierarchy
* node systems
* local transforms
* offsets
* attachment inheritance

## Practical Knowledge

* weapon construction
* furniture systems
* composite models
* pseudo-animation

## Optimization Knowledge

* attachment budgeting
* particle cost
* scaling tradeoffs

## Debugging Knowledge

* radians
* offsets
* coordinate spaces
* pose interaction

---

# 23. Best Practices

## Recommended

* use minimal attachments
* reuse node structures
* keep offsets organized
* document rotations
* test poses frequently

## Avoid

* excessive particle spam
* constant update loops
* giant invisible meshes
* attachment duplication

---

# 24. Important Mental Model

The most important concept:

Bloxd.io Entity Meshes are NOT standalone models.

They are:
VISUAL EXTENSIONS OF ENTITY SKELETON NODES.

Everything flows from that principle.

The entity moves.
The node moves.
The mesh follows.

That is the foundation of the entire system.

---

# 25. High-Level Summary

The Bloxd.io Entity Mesh System is effectively:

* a procedural attachment framework
* a lightweight skeletal visual system
* a pseudo-modeling toolkit
* a runtime compositing engine

It enables advanced visual engineering despite lacking traditional custom model importing.

Mastery comes from:

* understanding node hierarchy
* understanding transforms
* mastering offsets
* combining simple primitives creatively
* optimizing update behavior
* exploiting pose and animation interactions"
"Iron Watermelon
Type: Easter Egg Block / Legendary Collectible / Community Lore Character
The Iron Watermelon is one of the most famous secrets in Bloxd.io. It exists both as a real block that players can obtain and as a legendary ""mob"" or entity that became part of the game's community lore. Many players consider it the closest thing Bloxd.io has to a mascot creepypasta.
Iron Watermelon (Block)
Type: Rare Resource Block
Appearance: A normal watermelon with a white iron-colored texture instead of the usual green exterior.
How to Obtain:
Plant Watermelon Seeds.
Wait for watermelons to grow.
Extremely rarely, an Iron Watermelon will grow instead of a normal watermelon.
The commonly accepted chance is roughly 0.01% (about 1 in 10,000), although the exact rate has never been officially confirmed.
Mining Requirements:
Must be mined with a Pickaxe.
To obtain the block itself as an item, a Moonstone Pickaxe is generally required.
Breaking it incorrectly drops nothing.
Drops:
Produces 128 Iron Bars when broken or crafted down.
This makes it one of the most valuable farming rewards in the game.
Best Use:
Iron farming.
Rare-item collecting.
Decoration and prestige builds.
Trading and displaying wealth.
Iron Watermelon (Mob / Entity)
Type: Removed Secret Entity
The Iron Watermelon entity was a mysterious 2D jumpscare character reportedly added in the early days of Bloxd.io. It was never a normal mob like a deer or zombie. Instead, it functioned as a strange easter egg intended to surprise players who traveled beyond world limits.
Appearance:
White watermelon body.
Black eyes.
Straight expressionless mouth.
Red aura surrounding the face.
Completely 2-dimensional.
Behavior:
Did not attack.
Did not deal damage.
Could not be killed.
Only appeared as a jumpscare.
Where It Appeared:
Near old world boundaries.
Around 6,000+ blocks from spawn according to community reports.
Possibly at extreme build heights.
Current Status:
Removed from gameplay.
Preserved in community lore.
Referenced by loading-screen messages and other easter eggs.
Lore
The Iron Watermelon began as a community myth. Players noticed loading-screen messages such as:
""Ever seen a loading screen with a jump scare?""
""I heard there are secret mobs if you build high enough...""
Many players believed these messages referred to a hidden creature living beyond the world's limits. Over time, screenshots and stories spread throughout the community, creating the Iron Watermelon legend.
According to Bloxd.io lore pages, Arthur supposedly used the Iron Watermelon to discourage players from exploring beyond the old world borders before infinite terrain generation existed. If a player traveled too far, they might encounter the Iron Watermelon jumpscare and be disconnected.
Some lore sources even speculate that the Iron Watermelon is connected to Moonstones and the destruction of Bloxd's moon, although this is generally considered mythological in-universe lore rather than confirmed game canon.
Eventually, the developers turned the legend into a real easter egg by adding the Iron Watermelon block to farming. This means the creature technically no longer appears, but its block remains in the game as a tribute to one of Bloxd.io's oldest myths.
AI Training Summary
Iron Watermelon
Category: Secret Easter Egg
Exists As: Block and former Entity
Rarity: Extremely rare
Obtained From: Watermelon farming
Estimated Chance: ~1/10,000
Block Color: White/Iron-colored watermelon
Breaks Into: 128 Iron Bars
Best Tool: Pickaxe
Mob Status: Removed
Mob Damage: None
Mob Behavior: Jumpscare only
Lore Role: Guardian of old world borders / Bloxd.io myth
Community Importance: One of the most famous secrets in Bloxd.io history."

"Swords
Type: Melee Weapon
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond, Knight
Description: Swords are the primary melee weapons in Bloxd.io and are designed for direct close-range combat. They offer the highest consistent melee damage, fast attack speed, and strong PvP performance. Higher-tier variants deal more damage and are generally preferred for competitive combat.
Enchantable: Yes
Common Enchantments: Damage, Attack Speed, Critical Damage, Horizontal Knockback (Knight Sword exclusive)
Best Use: General PvP, PvE, dueling, and boss fights.
Bows
Type: Ranged Weapon
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond
Description: Bows fire arrows after being charged. They are effective at medium and long range and reward accuracy. Higher tiers increase damage output, allowing skilled players to weaken or eliminate enemies before they can engage in melee combat.
Enchantable: Currently no documented bow-specific enchantments.
Ammo Required: Arrows
Best Use: Long-range combat, hunting, support fire, and PvP engagements.
Crossbows
Type: Ranged Weapon
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond
Description: Crossbows function similarly to bows but can remain loaded after charging. This allows players to prepare a shot before combat begins. They generally emphasize ambush tactics and burst damage.
Enchantable: Currently no documented crossbow-specific enchantments.
Ammo Required: Arrows
Best Use: Ambushes, defensive positions, and precision attacks.
Spears
Type: Reach/Mobility Weapon
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond, Moonstone (special/unobtainable)
Description: Spears feature a forward lunge attack that propels the user toward the target. They sacrifice some raw damage compared to swords in exchange for increased reach and mobility. Spears are highly effective for aggressive playstyles and chasing opponents.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Special Mechanics: Forward dash/lunge attack.
Best Use: Mobility combat, chasing enemies, and closing distance quickly.
Daggers
Type: Stealth Weapon
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond, Moonstone (special/unobtainable)
Description: Daggers deal bonus damage when attacking from behind and gain additional effectiveness through repeated strikes. They are designed for stealth, flanking, and assassination-style gameplay.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Special Mechanics: Backstab bonus damage and combo-based damage scaling.
Best Use: Ambushes, stealth attacks, and hit-and-run combat.
Axes
Type: Heavy Melee Weapon / Tool
Variants: Wood, Stone, Iron, Gold, Diamond, Artisan, Moonstone (removed)
Description: Although primarily tools for chopping wood, axes can also be used as powerful melee weapons. They generally hit harder than most tools and can serve as emergency combat weapons.
Enchantable: Yes
Common Enchantments: Break Speed, Momentum, Lumber Aura
Best Use: Hybrid utility/combat gameplay.
Clubs
Type: Heavy Blunt Weapon
Variants: Multiple material tiers (varies by update)
Description: Clubs are blunt-force melee weapons intended to deliver strong close-range attacks. They emphasize raw power over speed and are generally used as alternatives to swords.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Best Use: Direct melee combat.
Maces
Type: Heavy Impact Weapon
Variants: Multiple material tiers (varies by update)
Description: Maces are heavy melee weapons that focus on delivering powerful strikes. Community testing suggests they benefit momentum-based attacks and reward aggressive movement.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Best Use: High-damage melee engagements.
Whips
Type: Reach Weapon
Variants: Multiple material tiers (varies by update)
Description: Whips offer longer attack range than most melee weapons. They allow users to attack while maintaining distance from opponents and are useful for controlling positioning during fights.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Best Use: Zoning, spacing, and reach-based combat.
Boomerangs
Type: Returning Projectile Weapon
Variants: Gold, Diamond, Moonstone
Description: Boomerangs are thrown weapons that return to the user after being launched. They can potentially strike enemies multiple times during a single throw and are effective for harassment and mobile combat.
Enchantable: No documented enchantments.
Special Mechanics: Returns after being thrown.
Best Use: Mobile ranged combat and repeated pressure.
Stick
Type: Knockback Weapon
Variants: None
Description: The Stick deals very little damage but produces extremely high knockback. It is commonly used to push opponents off platforms, bridges, and elevated structures.
Enchantable: No
Best Use: Knockback-focused combat.
Coconut
Type: Throwable Projectile
Variants: None
Description: A throwable item capable of dealing damage at range. It is often used for chip damage or harassment.
Enchantable: No
Best Use: Light ranged attacks.
Mango
Type: Utility Projectile
Variants: None
Description: A throwable item primarily used to apply knockback rather than direct damage.
Enchantable: No
Best Use: Crowd control and displacement.
Fat Cactus
Type: Improvised Weapon
Variants: None
Description: A normally environmental item that can be used offensively in certain situations.
Enchantable: No
Best Use: Emergency combat or novelty use.
Arrows
Type: Ammunition
Variants: Standard Arrow
Description: Arrows are the ammunition used by bows and crossbows. They are not weapons on their own but are required for ranged combat.
Enchantable: No
Best Use: Ammunition for ranged weapons.
AI Classification Summary
Melee Weapons: Swords, Spears, Daggers, Axes, Clubs, Maces, Whips
Ranged Weapons: Bows, Crossbows, Boomerangs
Utility/Knockback Weapons: Stick, Mango
Throwable Weapons: Coconut, Boomerang
Improvised Weapons: Fat Cactus
Ammunition: Arrows
Most Powerful Melee: Knight Sword
Best Stealth Weapon: Dagger
Best Mobility Weapon: Spear
Best Long-Range Weapon: Diamond Bow / Diamond Crossbow
Best Knockback Weapon: Stick
Best Reach Weapon: Whip
Best Hybrid Tool-Weapon: Axe"
"RPG (Rocket-Propelled Grenade) — Explosive Launcher
The RPG is a rocket launcher that fires explosive rockets in a straight trajectory. Upon impact, the rocket creates a large explosion that damages nearby players, produces significant knockback, and destroys terrain blocks. It is highly effective for destroying structures, attacking groups of enemies, and creating openings in defensive positions. It can also be used for ""rocket jumping,"" where players launch themselves into the air at the cost of taking damage. The RPG performs best in medium-range combat, terrain destruction, and area denial.
Super RPG — Heavy Explosive Launcher
The Super RPG is an upgraded version of the RPG with much greater explosive power. Its rockets deal extremely high damage, create larger explosions, and generate stronger knockback effects. The weapon is designed for maximum destruction and is often considered one of the strongest explosive weapons in the game. It performs best when clearing fortified positions, eliminating clustered enemies, and causing large-scale terrain damage.
Grenade Launcher — Arcing Explosive Launcher
The Grenade Launcher fires explosive projectiles that are affected by gravity. Unlike RPG rockets, the grenades travel in an arc and can bounce after the first explosion before exploding again. This allows the weapon to damage enemies hiding behind cover or in confined spaces. The Grenade Launcher excels in indirect fire, room clearing, and attacking enemies around corners or behind obstacles.
Frag Grenade — Throwable Explosive
The Frag Grenade is a handheld explosive that can be thrown and detonates after a short delay. It deals area-of-effect damage to nearby players and is intended for flushing enemies out of cover, softening enemy groups, or controlling space. It performs best in enclosed areas, chokepoints, and situations where enemies are clustered together.
Mining Grenade — Utility Explosive
The Mining Grenade is a throwable explosive primarily intended for breaking terrain and modifying the environment. While it can still damage players, its main purpose is creating tunnels, removing obstacles, and rapidly altering the battlefield. It performs best when terrain manipulation is more important than direct combat.
Bouncy Bomb — Throwable Explosive
The Bouncy Bomb is an explosive device that ricochets or bounces before detonating. This unpredictable movement allows it to reach enemies hiding behind cover or in difficult-to-access locations. It performs best in enclosed environments where rebounds can be used strategically.
Moonstone Explosive — Placed Explosive
The Moonstone Explosive is a placeable explosive block that detonates to damage players and destroy terrain. It functions similarly to a demolition charge and is useful for creating traps, breaching structures, and preparing ambushes. It performs best in defensive setups, base raiding, and planned explosions.
Remote Moonstone Explosive — Remote Detonation Explosive
The Remote Moonstone Explosive is a variant of the Moonstone Explosive that can be triggered remotely. This allows players to control exactly when the explosion occurs. It is particularly effective for ambushes, trap systems, defensive perimeters, and coordinated attacks. It performs best when timing and positioning are important.
Smoke Grenade — Tactical Utility Grenade
The Smoke Grenade creates a cloud of smoke that obscures vision rather than dealing damage. It is primarily a tactical tool used for concealment, escape, advancing across open areas, or confusing opponents. It performs best in objective-based modes and team coordination scenarios.
Quick Classification
Rocket Launchers: RPG, Super RPG
Explosive Launchers: Grenade Launcher
Throwable Damage Explosives: Frag Grenade, Bouncy Bomb
Terrain/Utility Explosives: Mining Grenade
Placed Explosives: Moonstone Explosive, Remote Moonstone Explosive
Tactical Grenades: Smoke Grenade"
"AK-47 (Assault Rifle)
The AK-47 is a balanced automatic assault rifle that performs well at nearly every range. It has good damage, decent accuracy, and a large magazine, making it one of the easiest weapons for beginners to use. It excels in medium-range fights and general-purpose combat where versatility is more important than specialization.
M16 (Assault Rifle)
The M16 is an automatic assault rifle with a large magazine and reliable damage output. It is designed for sustained fire and works best at medium range, where players can continuously pressure enemies without needing extremely precise aim. It is often considered a dependable all-round weapon.
TAR-21 (Assault Rifle)
The TAR-21 is a high-damage assault rifle that rewards accurate players. It hits harder than many other rifles and is effective at medium to long range. Its strength lies in quickly eliminating opponents before they can close the distance.
SKAR (Assault Rifle)
The SKAR is a powerful automatic rifle with strong damage and accuracy. It is particularly effective for players who prefer controlled bursts and medium-to-long-range engagements. It balances power and precision better than most fully automatic weapons.
MP40 (Submachine Gun)
The MP40 is a fast-firing SMG built for close-range combat. It sacrifices damage and range for mobility and rate of fire. It performs best when rushing enemies, clearing rooms, or fighting in tight spaces where accuracy matters less than speed.
Mini Izu (Submachine Gun)
The Mini Izu is another rapid-fire SMG focused on close-quarters combat. It allows aggressive movement and fast eliminations at short distances but becomes less effective as range increases.
M1911 (Pistol)
The M1911 is a semi-automatic handgun often used as a backup weapon. It offers respectable damage and quick handling. It is best for finishing weakened opponents or fighting when a primary weapon runs out of ammunition.
Block-19 (Pistol)
The Block-19 is a lightweight pistol with fast handling and decent accuracy. It serves as a reliable sidearm and is most useful in emergency situations or when mobility is important.
Deagle (Heavy Pistol)
The Deagle delivers very high damage per shot but requires accurate aim. It rewards headshots and precise shooting. It is best used by skilled players who can consistently land powerful hits.
AWP (Sniper Rifle)
The AWP is the iconic long-range sniper rifle. It deals massive damage and can eliminate enemies with very few shots. It excels at long-range engagements, defending positions, and picking off targets before they can approach.
VSR (Sniper Rifle)
The VSR is another sniper rifle focused on precision and long-distance combat. Compared to the AWP, it generally emphasizes accurate shots and consistent damage over raw stopping power. It is ideal for patient players who prefer positioning and accuracy.
DMR (Designated Marksman Rifle)
The DMR bridges the gap between assault rifles and sniper rifles. It is designed for accurate semi-automatic fire at medium-to-long range. It excels at repeatedly landing precise shots without the slow firing speed of a traditional sniper.
FMR (Burst Rifle)
The FMR fires a three-round burst each time the trigger is pulled. It combines high burst damage with good accuracy, making it extremely effective at medium range. Skilled players can eliminate opponents quickly by landing all three shots of a burst.
Double Barrel (Shotgun)
The Double Barrel is a high-damage shotgun designed for point-blank combat. It can deal devastating damage in a single blast but has limited ammunition before reloading. It is best for ambushes and extremely close encounters.
Striker-12 (Shotgun)
The Striker-12 is a combat shotgun with greater sustained fire than the Double Barrel. It dominates close-range fights and is excellent for clearing groups of enemies or holding narrow areas.
GPMG (Machine Gun)
The GPMG is a general-purpose machine gun with a large ammunition supply and strong suppressive fire capability. It is best for holding positions, controlling choke points, and continuously damaging multiple enemies. Mobility is usually sacrificed for firepower.
Minigun (Heavy Machine Gun)
The Minigun fires an enormous number of rounds and is intended for overwhelming enemies through sheer volume of fire. It performs best against groups and in defensive situations where mobility is less important.
One Shot Pistol (Special Pistol)
The One Shot Pistol is a powerful handgun capable of dealing extreme damage. It rewards accurate shooting and is intended for players who can consistently hit critical shots.
Grenade Launcher (Explosive Weapon)
The Grenade Launcher fires explosive projectiles that damage multiple enemies and can clear fortified positions. It excels against clustered targets and defensive structures.
RPG (Rocket Launcher)
The RPG launches explosive rockets that deal heavy area damage. It is most effective against groups of enemies and fortified positions but requires careful aiming due to slower projectiles.
Super RPG (Heavy Rocket Launcher)
The Super RPG is an upgraded explosive launcher with even greater destructive power. It is intended for maximum area damage and high-impact attacks.
Quick Classification
Assault Rifles: AK-47, M16, TAR-21, SKAR
Submachine Guns: MP40, Mini Izu
Pistols: M1911, Block-19, Deagle, One Shot Pistol
Sniper/Marksman Weapons: AWP, VSR, DMR
Burst Rifle: FMR
Shotguns: Double Barrel, Striker-12
Machine Guns: GPMG, Minigun
Explosive Weapons: Grenade Launcher, RPG, Super RPG"
