"Strength Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Plum. Grants the Damage effect, increasing damage by 35%. Strength II is crafted from Strength Potion + Moonstone and increases damage by 50%. Variants: Strength Potion, Strength Potion II, Splash Strength Potion, Splash Strength Potion II, Arrow of Strength.
Defense Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Pear. Grants Damage Reduction, reducing incoming damage by 40%. Defense II is crafted from Defense Potion + Moonstone and reduces damage by 50%. Variants: Defense Potion, Defense Potion II, Splash Defense Potion, Splash Defense Potion II, Arrow of Defense.
Speed Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Cracked Coconut. Increases movement speed, sprint speed, and jump distance by about 50%. Speed II is crafted from Speed Potion + Moonstone and boosts speed by about 120%. Variants: Speed Potion, Speed Potion II, Splash Speed Potion, Splash Speed Potion II, Arrow of Speed.
Jump Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Watermelon Slice. Raises jump height by 2 blocks. Jump II is crafted with an Iron Bar and raises jump height by 6 blocks. Variants: Jump Potion, Jump Potion II, Splash Jump Potion, Splash Jump Potion II, Arrow of Jumping.
Invisibility Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Melon Slice. Makes the player and equipped item invisible until the effect expires or the player attacks/takes damage. Variants: Invisibility Potion, Splash Invisibility Potion, Arrow of Invisibility. No II version exists.
Haste Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Full Bowl of Cranberries. Increases mining speed to roughly 2× normal. Haste II is crafted with an Iron Bar and increases mining speed to roughly 3× normal. Variants: Haste Potion, Haste Potion II, Splash Haste Potion, Splash Haste Potion II, Arrow of Haste.
Regeneration Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Cherry + Melon Slice. Restores approximately 5 health per second. Regeneration II restores approximately 8 health per second. Variants: Regeneration Potion, Regeneration Potion II, Splash Regeneration Potion, Splash Regeneration Potion II, Arrow of Regeneration.
Instant Healing Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Apple + Watermelon Slice. Instantly restores 30 health. Instant Healing II restores 50 health. Variants: Instant Healing Potion, Instant Healing Potion II, Splash Instant Healing Potion, Splash Instant Healing Potion II, Arrow of Instant Healing.
Instant Damage Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Cactus + Pumpkin Pie. Instantly deals 30 damage. Instant Damage II deals 60 damage. Variants: Instant Damage Potion, Instant Damage Potion II, Splash Instant Damage Potion, Splash Instant Damage Potion II, Arrow of Instant Damage.
Weakness Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Pumpkin. Causes the affected player to receive 20% more damage. Weakness II causes 30% more damage taken. Variants: Weakness Potion, Weakness Potion II, Splash Weakness Potion, Splash Weakness Potion II, Arrow of Weakness.
Poison Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Cactus. Deals approximately 5 damage per second. Poison II deals approximately 7 damage per second. Variants: Poison Potion, Poison Potion II, Splash Poison Potion, Splash Poison Potion II, Arrow of Poison.
Slowness Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Bread + Poppy. Reduces movement speed by 30%. Slowness II reduces movement speed by 40%. Variants: Slowness Potion, Slowness Potion II, Splash Slowness Potion, Splash Slowness Potion II, Arrow of Slowness.
Shield Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Corn. Grants 30 shield points. Shield II grants 40 shield points. Variants: Shield Potion, Shield Potion II, Splash Shield Potion, Splash Shield Potion II, Arrow of Shield.
Knockback Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Mango. Launches the target roughly 13 blocks upward. The upgraded splash version launches targets roughly 30 blocks upward. Variants: Knockback Potion, Splash Knockback Potion, Splash Knockback Potion II, Arrow of Knockback. No normal Knockback II potion exists.
Heat Resistance Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Chili Pepper. Prevents lava damage while active. Variants: Heat Resistance Potion, Splash Heat Resistance Potion, Arrow of Heat Resistance. No II version exists.
Double Jump Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + Banana. Grants a second jump while airborne. Variants: Double Jump Potion, Splash Double Jump Potion, Arrow of Double Jump. No II version exists.
Aura XP Potion — Crafted with Water Bottle + 3 Aura XP Orbs. Grants Aura XP levels. The wiki documents the base potion, while newer item lists also mention Aura XP Potion II and splash forms in some versions. Variants confirmed on the potion page: Aura XP Potion.
Milk Potion — Obtained by using an Empty Bottle on a cow. Removes all active potion effects. Variants: Milk Potion, Splash Milk Potion, Arrow of Milk. Cannot be upgraded.
X-Ray Vision Potion — Grants X-Ray Vision. The current item list shows X-Ray Vision Potion, Splash X-Ray Vision Potion, and Arrow of X-Ray Vision. No II version is listed.
Mining Yield Potion — Increases mining yield. Mining Yield II is also listed. Variants: Mining Yield Potion, Mining Yield Potion II, Splash Mining Yield Potion, Splash Mining Yield Potion II, Arrow of Mining Yield.
Brain Rot Potion — Crafted using Rotten Brain. Applies the Brain Rot effect. Variants: Brain Rot Potion, Splash Brain Rot Potion, Arrow of Brain Rot. Cannot be upgraded.
Chaos Potion — Special potion that gives two random positive effects and one random negative effect when consumed. Variants: Chaos Potion only. It cannot be upgraded.
this yields 22 base potion families: Strength, Defense, Speed, Jump, Invisibility, Haste, Regeneration, Instant Healing, Instant Damage, Weakness, Poison, Slowness, Shield, Knockback, Heat Resistance, Double Jump, Aura XP, Milk, X-Ray Vision, Mining Yield, Brain Rot, and Chaos."
"Plains — The Plains biome is one of the most common biomes in Bloxd.io. It consists of wide open grassy terrain with gentle hills, scattered maple trees, flowers, and naturally spawning crops. It is often considered the default biome and is a good location for gathering basic resources and building settlements.
Grassy Plains — Grassy Plains are similar to regular Plains but contain much denser vegetation. Large amounts of grass and tall grass cover the landscape, creating a greener and more overgrown appearance. Trees are relatively sparse, and visibility can be slightly reduced due to the heavy ground cover.
Meadows — Meadows are colorful grassland biomes covered with flowers and patches of cotton. Corn can naturally generate here, making the biome valuable for farming. The terrain is generally open and pleasant, with a bright, vibrant atmosphere.
Maple Forest — Maple Forests are dense woodlands dominated by maple trees. The thick tree coverage provides large amounts of wood and creates a classic forest environment. Small clearings occasionally appear between clusters of trees.
Pear Forest — Pear Forests are characterized by large numbers of pear trees mixed with some maple trees. Players can gather pears as a food resource while also collecting wood. The biome resembles a fruit orchard spread across a natural forest.
Aspen Forest — Aspen Forests contain many aspen trees, which give the biome a distinct appearance compared to maple forests. Maple trees may also appear, but aspens dominate the landscape. The forest is moderately dense and rich in wood resources.
Plum Forest — Plum Forests feature plum trees growing throughout the biome alongside maple trees. The biome serves as a source of plums and wood while maintaining a traditional forest feel. The mixture of tree types creates visual variety.
Bluebell Forest — Bluebell Forests are rare and visually distinctive. The ground is heavily covered with bluebell flowers, creating a blue-tinted forest floor beneath the trees. This biome is known more for its beauty than for unique resources.
Autumn Forest — Autumn Forests resemble maple forests during fall. Trees display orange and yellow foliage, while fallen leaves and autumn ferns cover much of the ground. The biome has a warm seasonal appearance unlike any other forest type.
Cherry Blossom Forest — Cherry Blossom Forests are filled with cherry trees bearing pink blossoms. Cherry leaves are often scattered across the terrain, giving the biome a colorful and decorative look. It is one of the most visually recognizable biomes in the game.
Desert — Deserts consist primarily of sand with little vegetation. Cacti commonly grow throughout the biome, and chili peppers may occasionally be found. Water and trees are scarce, making survival more resource-dependent.
Cactus Fields — Cactus Fields are a rare desert variant where cacti grow in unusually high concentrations. The landscape remains sandy, but the sheer density of cacti makes the biome stand out from normal deserts and provides an abundant cactus supply.
Red Desert — Red Deserts function similarly to standard deserts but are composed mainly of red sand instead of normal sand. The biome has a striking reddish appearance that makes it easy to identify from a distance.
Snowy Plains — Snowy Plains are cold grassland regions covered in snow. Cranberries can be found growing here, and occasional maple trees survive in the harsh climate. The biome is open and bright but much colder in appearance than standard plains.
Snowy Pine Forest — Snowy Pine Forests are pine forests blanketed in snow. Pine trees dominate the landscape, and the snowy ground creates a winter atmosphere. The biome provides plentiful wood while maintaining a cold environment.
Snowy Cedar Forest — Snowy Cedar Forests are the snowy counterpart of Cedar Forests. Cedar trees grow across the snow-covered terrain, creating a rare and picturesque winter woodland. Wood resources are abundant, though the biome itself is uncommon.
Frozen Badlands — Frozen Badlands are among the most dangerous and rewarding biomes in Bloxd.io. Massive ice spikes rise from the terrain, and special hostile creatures can appear. The biome may contain graves, skull temples, and valuable ores, making it attractive for exploration despite the risks.
Spectral Forest — Spectral Forests are mysterious biomes filled with spectral trees. Ancient ruins may generate throughout the area, contributing to the biome's eerie atmosphere. The unusual vegetation and structures make it one of the game's most distinctive locations.
Pine Forest — Pine Forests are dense forests dominated by tall pine trees. Wildlife such as deer, bears, and wolves may be encountered here. The biome is a major source of wood and offers a more rugged wilderness feel than most other forests.
Cedar Forest — Cedar Forests are considered the rarest forest biome in Bloxd.io. Cedar trees dominate the landscape, growing across grassy terrain with relatively little variation. Their rarity makes them valuable to explorers seeking unique environments.
Jungle — Jungles are dense tropical biomes packed with vegetation. Jungle trees, mango trees, and palm trees grow throughout the area, while waterfalls and jungle structures can sometimes be found. The biome is rich in resources but can be difficult to navigate due to its thick plant growth.
Complete Bloxd.io biome list (21 total): Plains, Grassy Plains, Meadows, Maple Forest, Pear Forest, Aspen Forest, Plum Forest, Bluebell Forest, Autumn Forest, Cherry Blossom Forest, Desert, Cactus Fields, Red Desert, Snowy Plains, Snowy Pine Forest, Snowy Cedar Forest, Frozen Badlands, Spectral Forest, Pine Forest, Cedar Forest, and Jungle."
"# Super Rank
Super Rank is a paywall in Bloxd.io that gives players certain cosmetics and an ad-free experience. It was added by Arthur on December 18, 2024.

## Perks
You will receive the following perks after you paid for the Super Rank:

 - The option to customize the colour of your name above your character’s head and in the chat
 - Capes as player customisation options
 - The Super Rank nametag next to your name above your character’s head, in the chat, and in the friends tab of other players that you are friends with
 - No advertisements
Other than that, Super Rank does not give you advantages, boosts or perks in any gamemodes.

Each Super Rank subscription lasts for 30 days (previously 90), and you need to renew the subscription if you want to have Super Rank for a longer period.

## Gifting
Since February 7, 2025, you can gift friends Super Rank on the condition that you pay for them.

## CrazyGames
From February 7, you can buy Super Rank on CrazyGames.

## Security Exploit Bounties
From December 11, 2025, you can receive one year of Super Rank for free if you reported a security flaw in Bloxd.io (bugs that could crash Bloxd for everyone), and 3-6 months for more minor issues.

## Name Tags
When you have Super Rank, there will be a “Super” next to your name in all the messages you send in the chat.

## Trivia
 - The Super Rank advertisement appears on the home page and in B-menus (the menu accessed by pressing the shopping cart icon or B).
 - Upon purchasing Super Rank, you will be redirected to https://bloxd.io/super-rank-welcome. You can also access the link without having Super Rank (but you will not get it from the link)."
"Here is a Bloxd mod, uploaded on Bloxdhub, by the user mustarddd
titile: NOT ENOUGH ANIMATIONS!!
desc: Bloxd has a lack of player animations so here are ones i made. List of animations : idle breathing, walking, Naruto run (sprinting), climbing ladder, falling & landing from high spot, spear lunge, eating & drinking, swimming.

My personal 2 favorites are the Naruto run and the swimming animation

Shoutout to this mod https://bloxdhub.com/mod/2068 cuz i discovered of the existence of api animate entity from it.

World Code:
playerStates = {};

/* --- COMBAT & CONSUME EVENTS --- */

onPlayerClick = (id, wasAlt) => {
    var now = api.now();
    if (!playerStates[id]) playerStates[id] = {};
    playerStates[id].mode = ""none""; /* Refresh trigger */

    var held = api.getHeldItem(id);
    if (held && held.name.includes(""Spear"") && !wasAlt) {
        playerStates[id].animLock = now + 700;
        playerStates[id].mode = ""combat"";
        api.animateEntity(id, {
            animationDurationMs: 600, loop: false,
            nodeAnimations: {
                TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.2, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.3, rotation: { point: [1.0, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0] } }] },
                ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.8, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 0.3, rotation: { point: [-2.0, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, -0.07] } }] },
                ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0.3, rotation: { point: [0.8, 0, 0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0.07] } }] }
            }
        }, 0, 1.0);
    }
};

onPlayerStartChargingItem = (id, itemName) => {
    if (!playerStates[id]) playerStates[id] = {};
    var isPotion = itemName.includes(""Potion"") || itemName.includes(""Bottle"") || itemName.includes(""Milk"");
    playerStates[id].isEating = true;

    if (isPotion) {
        api.animateEntity(id, {
            animationDurationMs: 400, loop: true,
            nodeAnimations: {
                TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.3, 0, 0] } }] },
                HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.7, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.9, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-0.7, 0, 0] } }]},
                ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-2.2, 0, 0.6] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-2.5, 0, 0.8] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-2.2, 0, 0.6] } }]}
            }
        });
    } else {
        api.animateEntity(id, {
            animationDurationMs: 350, loop: true,
            nodeAnimations: {
                TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.15, 0, 0] } }] },
                HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.3, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.45, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0.3, 0, 0] } }]},
                ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-1.4, 0, 0.7] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-1.6, 0, 0.9] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-1.4, 0, 0.7] } }]}
            }
        });
    }
};

onPlayerFinishChargingItem = (id) => {
    if (playerStates[id]) { playerStates[id].isEating = false; playerStates[id].mode = ""none""; }
};

/* --- MAIN TICK LOOP --- */

tick = (ms) => {
    var ids = api.getPlayerIds();
    var now = api.now();

    for (var i = 0; i < ids.length; i++) {
        var id = ids[i];
        if (!playerStates[id]) playerStates[id] = { mode: ""none"", fallStartTime: 0, wasHighFall: false, landLock: 0, animLock: 0, isEating: false };
        var s = playerStates[id];

        /* Priority Locks: If eating, landing, or fighting, skip movement anims */
        if (s.isEating || now < s.landLock || now < s.animLock) continue;

        var vel = api.getVelocity(id);
        var horizSpeed = vel[0] * vel[0] + vel[2] * vel[2];
        var totalSpeed = horizSpeed + vel[1] * vel[1];
        var standingOn = api.getBlockCoordinatesPlayerStandingOn(id);
        var inAir = (standingOn && standingOn.length === 0);

        /* Environment Checks */
        var units = api.getUnitCoordinatesLifeformWithin(id);
        var onLadder = false, inWater = false;
        for (var u = 0; u < units.length; u++) {
            var b = api.getBlock(units[u][0], units[u][1], units[u][2]);
            if (b.includes(""Ladder"")) onLadder = true;
            if (b.includes(""Water"")) inWater = true;
        }

        /* --- 1. SWIMMING (BREASTSTROKE) --- */
        if (inWater) {
            api.setPlayerPose(id, ""standing"");
            if (totalSpeed > 0.1) {
                if (s.mode !== ""swimming"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 1400, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, 0] } }] },
                            HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-1.57, 0, 0] } }] },
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.6, 0, -0.6] } }, { timeFraction: 0.3, rotation: { point: [0.2, 0, -1.8] } }, { timeFraction: 0.6, rotation: { point: [-1.2, 0, -0.6] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [1.6, 0, -0.6] } }] },
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.6, 0, 0.6] } }, { timeFraction: 0.3, rotation: { point: [0.2, 0, 1.8] } }, { timeFraction: 0.6, rotation: { point: [-1.2, 0, 0.6] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [1.6, 0, 0.6] } }] },
                            LegRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, -0.1] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [1.8, 0, -0.4] } }, { timeFraction: 0.8, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, -0.1] } }]},
                            LegLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, 0.1] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [1.8, 0, 0.4] } }, { timeFraction: 0.8, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, 0.1] } }]}
                        }
                    }, 0.1, 1.0); s.mode = ""swimming"";
                }
            } else {
                if (s.mode !== ""swim_idle"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, { animationDurationMs: 200, loop: false, nodeAnimations: { 
                        TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, 0] } }] },
                        HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-1.57, 0, 0] } }] },
                        ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.0, 0, -0.6] } }] },
                        ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.0, 0, 0.6] } }] },
                        LegRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, -0.15] } }] },
                        LegLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.57, 0, 0.15] } }] }
                    }}, 0.1, 1.0); s.mode = ""swim_idle"";
                }
            }
            continue;
        }

        /* --- 2. CLIMBING --- */
        if (onLadder) {
            api.setPlayerPose(id, ""standing"");
            if (totalSpeed > 0.1) {
                if (s.mode !== ""climbing"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 600, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.3, 0, 0] } }] },
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-1.8, 0, 0.2] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, 0.2] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-1.8, 0, 0.2] } }] },
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, -0.2] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-1.8, 0, -0.2] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, -0.2] } }] }
                        }
                    }, 0.1, 1.0); s.mode = ""climbing"";
                }
            } else {
                if (s.mode !== ""climb_idle"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, { animationDurationMs: 200, loop: false, nodeAnimations: { 
                        TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0.3, 0, 0] } }] },
                        ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-1.2, 0, 0.2] } }] },
                        ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-1.2, 0, -0.2] } }] }
                    }}, 0.1, 1.0); s.mode = ""climb_idle"";
                }
            }
            continue;
        }

        /* --- 3. FALLING & LANDING --- */
        if (inAir) {
            if (vel[1] > 0) s.fallStartTime = 0; else if (s.fallStartTime === 0) s.fallStartTime = now;
            if (now - s.fallStartTime > 800 && vel[1] < -0.1) {
                if (s.mode !== ""falling"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 400, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.4, 0, 0] } }] },
                            HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.3, 0, 0] } }] },
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-3.1, 0, -0.07] } }] },
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-3.1, 0, 0.07] } }] }
                        }
                    }, 0.05, 1.0); s.mode = ""falling""; s.wasHighFall = true;
                }
            }
        } else {
            if (s.wasHighFall) {
                api.animateEntity(id, {
                    animationDurationMs: 250, loop: false,
                    nodeAnimations: {
                        TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.6, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0] } }] },
                        ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.4, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, -0.07] } }] },
                        ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.4, 0, 0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0.07] } }] }
                    }
                }, 0, 1.0);
                s.landLock = now + 250; s.wasHighFall = false; s.mode = ""none"";
            } else if (horizSpeed > 42) {
                /* --- 4. NINJA RUN --- */
                if (s.mode !== ""ninja"") {
                    api.setPlayerPose(id, ""standing"");
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 350, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.25, rotation: { point: [0.95, 0, 0.15] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.75, rotation: { point: [0.95, 0, -0.15] } }, { timeFraction: 1.0, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, 0] } }]},
                            HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, 0] } }] },
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.0, 0, -0.07] } }] },
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [1.0, 0, 0.07] } }] },
                            LegRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.9, 0, -0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, -0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 1.0, rotation: { point: [-0.9, 0, -0.05] } }]},
                            LegLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, 0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.9, 0, 0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 1.0, rotation: { point: [0.9, 0, 0.05] } }]}
                        }
                    }, 0, 1.0); s.mode = ""ninja"";
                }
            } else if (horizSpeed > 0.01) {
                /* --- 5. EXPRESSIVE WALK --- */
                if (s.mode !== ""walk"") {
                    api.setPlayerPose(id, ""standing"");
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 800, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.1, 0, -0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.1, 0, 0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0.1, 0, -0.05] } }]},
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.5, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0.5, 0, -0.07] } }]},
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, 0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.5, 0, 0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-0.5, 0, 0.07] } }]},
                            LegRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [-0.6, 0, -0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.6, 0, -0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [-0.6, 0, -0.05] } }]},
                            LegLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0.6, 0, 0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.6, 0, 0.05] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0.6, 0, 0.05] } }]}
                        }
                    }, 0, 1.0); s.mode = ""walk"";
                }
            } else {
                /* --- 6. BREATHING (IDLE) --- */
                if (s.mode !== ""idle"") {
                    api.animateEntity(id, {
                        animationDurationMs: 2500, loop: true,
                        nodeAnimations: {
                            TorsoNode: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [-0.06, 0, 0] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0] } }]},
                            HeadMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0.04, 0, 0] } }]},
                            ArmRightMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0, 0, -0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0, 0, -0.16] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, -0.07] } }]},
                            ArmLeftMesh: { timeline: [{ timeFraction: 0, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0.07] } }, { timeFraction: 0.5, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0.16] } }, { timeFraction: 1, rotation: { point: [0, 0, 0.07] } }]}
                        }
                    }, 0.1, 1.0); s.mode = ""idle"";
                }
            }
            s.fallStartTime = 0;
        }
    }
};

onPlayerLeave = (id) => { delete playerStates[id]; };

please use this knowledge so that you will understand how api.animateEntity is used and how to create animations using the API call. Also note the title and description, so that you will better understand how users usually make their mod's title and descriptions."

"Gameplay
The Bloxd SMP is the same as Survival, but with a trading system and other features.

Spawn
There is an island at spawn with some facilities on it.

Portal
Walking into the portal teleports you to a random location in the world, also called ""RTP"". This can teleport you to any biome in the game, from frozen badlands to plains to caves.

Coins leaderboard
The character models of the three players currently playing in the lobby with the most coins is shown on a leaderboard.

Item Marketplace
There are four NPCs in stalls at spawn, as well as some Crates beside them. These NPCs are used for various functions (see below).

Currency
Coins are used to buy items in the marketplace. You can earn coins by selling your items or fulfilling other players’ orders in the game. Your balance is visible to all players.

Selling
You can sell the resources you collected for Gold Coins at the “instant sell” NPC at spawn, or at the “sell” menu of the B-menu. You can sell three ways, instant sell, beng the worst in the current Bloxd economy, market, where you can set prices for people to buy, and orders, where you instantly get money for goods that the person wants, but only the cost that the person offers per item. The market is by far the best way to make money. To sell something on the market, click ""B"" and go to the market page. Then, search the item your willing to sell. Once on that page, if you have something to sell,

Buying
You can choose to sell some of your items, for a price set by yourself, to other players through the “market” NPC at spawn. You can also search for offers made by other players at the same NPC, and buy them if desirable.put the quantity and price per item.

Orders
You can request for other players to send you items, on the condition that you pay coins to them, through the “orders” NPC at spawn. You can also fulfil other player’s orders.

Duels
You can engage in 1v1 fights with other players by clicking on the “duels” NPC at spawn. When you click on him, you will be teleported to an 1v1 arena where you will fight other players from other lobbies. If nobody comes in the 1v1 fight lobby after a while, you will be teleported back to the SMP. If you die in a 1v1 fight, you will lose all your items.

Restrictions
As of now, players cannot craft protectors, and tribe creation is disabled."

It can be because you have no internet, weak internet, switched to another internet, enabled airplane mode, [alt] + [tab] for more than 5 minutes or switched off your device while in the server. Under any of these conditions, the game kicks you so that it doesn't think that you are lagging.

This is a bug.

Error:
Unable to create vertex buffer

Please refresh the page.
The game becomes disjointed, which causes it to unable to create vertices (Bloxd.io is made out of cubes, which are made out of squares. To make squares, you need triangles. There are 6 squares in a cube, and 2 triangles in a square. Triangles are made out of three angles, and each angle needs a vertex.)
You disconnected from the server.
Either your internet isn't working or this is a bug.

happening.

"There were special updates released during a certain time of each year, being April Fools, Halloween, and Christmas.

April Fools
2023
Bloxd.io was renamed to CraftMine.
Greenville was renamed to ""My Tiny Pony"" with the subtext ""Island Fairy Adventure"".
2024
Arthur announced in Discord that they will call themselves CubeAI and solely make AI agents for Minecraft buildings.
Aile (an admin of the Discord server) said, ""Beware: Apparently Players Running In Lobbies Fast On Ores Loses Storage.""
2025
The Developers said that “in recognition of international left-handed day” (which is ironically, in 13 August), all the controls are reversed.
2026
The number 67 was added to Bloxd.io on the home page.
A “67” icon was added to all name tags, regardless they are of players’.
Forget-me-nots were replaced with “67-me-nots”, which used the model six seven brainrot from Tsunami Steal Brainrots. However, its icon in the inventory showed the raw textures.
In the Discord, nearly all of the developers’ profile name “67” in it. Slushie and pegasys has 67 in their profile images, pegasys has drawn “6 7” on his profile picture while Slushie used the animated 67 emoji in his profile respectively.
When you try to join the Discord server, Bob’s eye are replaced with Six Seven statues.

A new home screen background was added.

The eyes of Bob on the home page changed to black and blue.

"Survival With Guns
Official Description: ""Survival with craftable guns and armed mobs""
Developed by Pegasys
Script Name: `classic_survivalGuns`
Tag: Limited Time
(NOTE: The article used to make this note is not 100% complete. Some information may be lacking. This also talks about a Limited Time game mode, which may or may not be removed, depending on player votes.)
Survival with Guns is a Gamemode added to Staging on May 8, 2026, and was added to the main game on May 11. It shares similar features with Survival, but allows guns to be crafted, increasing the difficulty.

The name “Survival with Guns” is self-explanatory: it has all the features of Sandbox Survival but additionally, guns can now be crafted, and hostile mobs use guns to attack.

"This page is about the official lore in Bloxd.io. This page is created as the developers said that they’re going to add a lot of lore in 2026.

Rules for contributing lore
Add sources for all lore. If the lore is from Discord, paste the complete message, with the author and the date, on here, or put a screenshot from Discord. If the lore appears in gameplay, put a screenshot from Bloxd.io. The hotbar should be in the screenshot to show that it is not cropped excessively.
Creepypasta is not lore, except when it is considered as lore by the devs. (Don’t put stuff from the Creepypasta wiki!)
Do not write lore that are not confirmed, created, or mentioned by the developers.
Lore in Worlds lobbies and Custom Games are not official; the lobbies are created by players.
When writing theories, avoid stretching the story to events, characters, places or whatever things that are not canon.
Lore is not derived from normal game mechanics.

Main lore (world of bloxd.io)
Once upon a time in the world of bloxd.io there was a moon, and it exploded and its fragments fell to Earth, becoming the moonstone ore. Because of this, there is no change of day and night in the world. Golems were created from stone and moonstone. According to the developers, there is magic in moonstonе that is involved in teleportation.

Fishing
Draugr Survivor
In the current Fishing gamemode, there is a cave at the other side of Gloomtide island. A “draugr survivor” can be in the cave. Its appearance is identical to the zombie skin in Infection, and the words “cursed salesman” (with each letter spaced far apart) written in “cursed text” can be seen under the words “draugr survivor” above its head. Before 10 January, clicking on the survivor will cause the message “Grr, come back later” to appear on the screen and in the chat.

Now, clicking on the Draugr Survivor will open the “cursed salesman” menu in the shop that offers a cursed rod for 8000 coins.

Aether Minnow and the sinkhole in Eldermire
Also in the latest Fishing gamemode, there is a fish called the “Aether Minnow”, which is a reference to a popular mod (the Aether) in Minecraft. its description is: “a tiny shimmering minnow that glows faintly, said to feed on magical residue seeping from an ancient sinkhole.”

Coincidentally, there is a sinkhole 103 blocks deep in the middle of Eldermire, and Aether Minnows are commonly caught in Eldermire.

Meteor Impact
In the second release of Fishing, going to the side of Sunhaven will result in finding an area where Mossy Messy Stone are Rocky Dirt are scattered on the ground, creating a dent, and Jungle and Normal Grass Blocks scatters the sea floor, as well with more Mossy Messy Stone at the end of the trail. There are some Moonstone Ores on the trail too, and at the end of the trail, there is a Block of Moonstone, representing the core. Using this information, the impact might have came a large fragment of a moon that had been turned into a meteor, which then landed here, leaving a visible trail.

After March 18, the meteorite can be seen directly, consisting compressed messy stone and blocks of moonstone.

Infection
The “Subway”, “Airport” and “Farm” map are chronologically related.

In Subway, the humans start from the underground carpark of Bloxd Hotel and ends at a subway station (Arthur Station).
In Airport, the humans start from a subway station of an airport and ends at a Bloxd Airlines plane.
In Farm, the humans start from a crashed Bloxd Airlines plane and ends at a truck in a farmhouse.

One Block
One Village “Bank robbery”
Behind the bank in One Village, there is a hole in the wall that leads to the vault of the bank, along with a ladder leading up to the hole. Some contents of the vault can also be seen scattered outside the hole. However, the vault is not emptied of contents, and Bling Bling (the banker) is not aware of this through his dialogues."
"Bloxd Item APIs

This page contains a list of item API's that you can use to make things like illegal item schematics, custom items, and more!

Custom crafting recipes
Note: the words in bold are supposed to be changed:

api.editItemCraftingRecipes(myId, ""Item Name"", [

   {

       requires: [

           {items: [""Item Name""], amt: Num},

           {items: [""Item Name""], amt: Num}

       ],

       produces: Num,

       station: ""Station Name""

   }

]);

If you want to include more items in the crafting recipe, put a comma next to the {items: [""Item Name""], amt: Num} and then press Enter and copy the [""Item Name""], amt: Num}.

“Item name” means any item.

“Station name” means any utility that can be used to craft items in Bloxd.io. Currently, they are the workbench, artisan bench, furnace and potion table

“Num” means any number.

Custom items
api.giveItem(myId, ""Item Name"", 1, {customAttributes: {enchantments: {""Enchant Name"": Num},enchantmentTier: ""EnchantTier""},customDisplayName: ""Custom Item Name"",customDescription: ""Custom Description"",})

   

List of attributes:

customDisplayName:
Enchantments:
customDescription
List of available enchants:

Damage
Critical Damage
Attack Speed
Break Speed
Momentum
Mining Yield
Mining Aura
Lumber Aura
Digging Aura
Farming Aura
Quick Charge
Arrow Speed
Arrow Damage
Health
Health Regen
Protection
Lumber Yield
Horizontal Knockback
Vertical Knockback
Knockback Resist
Stomp Damage
Place block(s) automatically
api.setBlock(x, y, z, ""Block Name"");

Replace Block Name with any Block, Make sure it is a Block. It does not work with items.

use let [x, y, z] = thisPos before you put api.setBlock(x, y, z, ""Block Name""); to make it set in the position the code block was put.

Example:

let [x, y, z] = thisPos

api.setBlock(x, y, z, ""Block of Emerald"");"
"some more world builder info, which is not explicitly stated in an official source but is still important info:

if a block name consist of two or more words, you must wrap the name in Qutation marks. 
Ex:
//replace ""Grass Block"" Stone
//set ""Maple Wood Planks""
If the name is only one word, you do not have to use quotation marks."
"WorldBuilder is a feature in Worlds lobbies in Bloxd.io that is used to edit large numbers of blocks in a short time. This can be useful in building large projects.

Commands
//help Gives you a list of WorldBuilder commands
//pos1 (coordinates) and //pos2 (coordinates) - sets position 1 and position 2, and the area between it (called “area” below). If you did not put in the coordinates, the position(s) will be set at where you are standing.
//set ""[block name]"" - Set the area with a specific block.
//fill ""[block name]"" - Same function as //set command
//copy - Copy the blocks in the area
//paste (coordinates) - Paste the area in a specific position
//replace [name of block that you want to replace] [name of block that you want it to be replaced with] - Replace the block(s) in the area.
//schematic save - Save the area to your device as a schematic file
//schematic load - Load a schematic file into your lobby.
//schematic list - List your schematics
//rotate (angle) (axis) — rotates the clipboard (set using the //copy command) by a certain angle on a certain axis. Default is 90 degrees on the y-axis
//flip [axis] — flips the clipboard on a certain axis
//shift (coordinates) — shifts the blocks in the area (set by //pos1 and //pos2) to the coordinates
//stack [count] [axis] — duplicates the blocks in the area by a certain number of times along a certain axis
//wand — give yourself a WorldBuilder wand to set position 1 and position 2 quickly
//clear — sets the area to air
//natrualize — converts all grass blocks, dirt and stone in the area to its natural layers
//creative — toggles creative mode for yourself"
"Custom Games
Info:
Description:
Check out games published by players!
Developer:
Oliver
Script Name:
(Invite Link:
`custom-games`)
(True Script:
`customGames`)
Custom Games are a large group of games made by players.


Contents
1	Wiki about Custom Games
2	Categories of custom games
3	How to make a custom game
4	History
5	Gallery
5.1	Pictures
5.2	Screenshots
6	References
Wiki about Custom Games
You can explore different Custom Games, how to play them and more in the Bloxd Custom Games Wiki. (https://breezewiki.com/bloxd-custom-games/wiki/Bloxd_Custom_Games_Wiki )

Categories of custom games
Custom Games are further categorised into Minigames Games, PvP Games, Simulator Games, Parkour Obby Games, Racing Games, and PvE Games. Games of each category have the “Minigames”, “PvP”, “Simulator”, and “Parkour Obby”, “Racing”, and “Survival” tag respectively, along with the common tag “Player Crafted”.

You can find these categories on the main menu under the thumbnails of the official game modes. The 8 games with the most number of active players from each category are shown in the main menu, while you can find the rest of the games by clicking on the arrow next to the names of the categories (to see the list of games). Games that does not belong to any category can still be found in the list of “custom games”.

How to make a custom game
You can build your games in a Worlds lobby, and submit a schematic that could become a playable minigame by using the command //schematic list if you are the owner or co-owner of the lobby.

History
Bloxd.io
May 1, 2025[1]	Custom Games is added to Bloxd.io
Discord channel #custom-games is created for discussions about custom games.
June 5, 2025	Added Spawn Protection toggle for Custom Games. When enabled, lobby spawn will be protected and no building/breaking/PvP will be allowed.
World lobbies can now be exported by lobby owners as Custom Games by using the ""Export as Custom Game"" setting in the owner menu of the shop or by using the //exportworld command.
June 10, 2025	Settings of Custom Games can now be edited, by using either the ""Edit"" button on your published schematic page or by using the //schematic list command.
Fall damage and world borders can now be enabled in Custom Games.
June 12, 2025[2]	Custom Games thumbnail updated.
Staging.bloxd.io
April 30, 2025	Player Crafted Games is added to staging.bloxd.io
May 1, 2025	Player Crafted Games is renamed to Custom Games
Script name is changed from player-published-games to custom-games
April 25,2026	Users can now upload custom thumbnails to for their custom games"
"Bedwars 4v4:
Official Desc:
A Ranked PVP Bedwars game where four-player teams battle on floating islands, defend their bed, and break enemy beds to win the match.
Developed by Harry
Script Name: `bedwars_4v4`
Tags: RANKED

Bedwars 4v4 is a ranked sub-gamemode of Bedwars. It is the fifth Ranked gamemode to be added to Bloxd, and the only Ranked gamemode that isn't part of 1v1 Fights. This gamemode was added on February 4, 2026.


Contents
1	Gameplay
2	List of ranks
3	Trivia
4	Tips
Gameplay
The gameplay is identical to Bedwars (destroy the other team’s bed, and then kill the opponents to win). However, there are two teams of four players competing against each other, and everyone has a MMR score that changes with how well you play. MMR scores are similar to elo systems, when you win you gain MMR however if you lose you lose MMR. When you achive higher ranks you start to gain less MMR per win and lose more MMR per loss.

Trivia
The gamemode's description is almost the same as the one for Squads, with the only differences being that Squad up in bedwars, a pvp game is replaced with A ranked pvp bedwars game and sky islands is replaced with floating islands.
Tips
If you want to get materials faster in the late game, invest 24 coal for a Pickaxe and mine the ores that spawn.
You can immediately rush the opponent base, because they are all probably trying to get the resources. Unless the game you are playing have a lot of sweaty players of course.
The less teamates in the generator at one time, the faster the resources spawn.
Blocking in on the other team's bed is a great strategy to break it, simply place blocks around you and start mining their defense, this will cause them to have to break blocks before they can hit you giving you time to break their bed!
Tools such as Pickaxes, Axes, and Shears can be extremely useful for breaking beds quickly!"
"Spikes are blocks in Bloxd.io that deals damage to players in contact of it. They were added to the game on October 21, 2024.


Contents
1	Obtaining
2	Variants
2.1	Wood Spikes
2.2	Stone Spikes
2.3	Iron Spikes
2.4	Gold Spikes
2.5	Diamond Spikes
2.6	Kill Spikes
3	99 Nights
4	Spike traps
5	Trivia
6	Gallery
6.1	Renders
Obtaining
All variants of spikes (except for the kill spikes) can be crafted using a workbench and the respective ores.

Stone Spikes can spawn in Structures in the Frozen Badlands biome and also the Ruin Structure in the Jungle.

Variants
Wood Spikes
This variant of Spikes does the lowest damage. It is crafted with 2 Wood Planks and 2 Sticks. This Spike deals 26 damage.

Stone Spikes
This variant of Spikes does more damage than the Wooden Spike. It is crafted from 2 Wood Spikes and a Messy Stone. This Spike deals 32 damage.

Iron Spikes
This variant of Spikes does more damage than the Stone Spike. It is crafted from 2 Wood Spikes and an Iron Bar. This Spike deals 40 damage.

Gold Spikes
This variant of Spikes does more damage than the Iron Spike. It is crafted from 2 Wood Spikes and a Gold Bar. This Spike deals 44 damage.

Diamond Spikes
This variant of Spikes does the most damage. It is crafted from 2 Wood spikes and a Diamond. This Spike deals 48 damage.

Kill Spikes
This variant of Spikes can only be obtained in the creative mode of Worlds. Kill spikes deal 100000 damage per second, which is 1000 times higher than the player’s health. This makes it as if kill spikes can kill players instantly.

99 Nights
In 99 Nights spikes can only be crafted from wood and the material that you want to spike to be made of.

Spike traps
The cross-sectional diagram of an example of a spike trap
The cross-sectional diagram of an example of a spike trap
You can build a “spike trap” such that players who walk into the trap will be damaged by spikes and cannot get out. Spike traps are built by digging a pit 2 or more blocks deep in the ground, putting spikes at the bottom of the hole, and covering the hole, and preferably the blocks near the hole, with carpets. Carpets do not have a hitbox, and players can fall through them.

Trivia
You cannot obtain Kill Spikes in the public sandbox lobby modes, but only in Creative Mode in Worlds lobbies. This may be because Kill Spikes were overpowered.
On Discord, Arthur posted an image of 6 potential designs of Wood Spikes in it before the update. There is also a player named ""Patricson_Bloxd"" in the image.
In Greenville, Spikes can damage a player even when PvP is not switched on at a player’s house.
The first day when spikes are added, coming in contact with multiple spikes will multiply the amount of damage dealt by the number of spikes you are in contact with. This “feature” was removed since the day after."
"The bed is a utility block in Bloxd.io that can be used to set spawn points and give perks to players by sleeping.


Contents
1	Functions
1.1	Setting spawn points
1.2	Rested Perks
2	Usage
2.1	Survival, peaceful, creative, worlds and custom games
2.2	Bedwars
2.3	Lucky towers
2.4	Pirates
2.5	Greenville
3	Recipes
4	Uses in Parkour
4.1	Block Clutching
5	Variants
6	Trivia
7	Half Beds
7.1	Custom Lobby Blocks
8	Gallery
8.1	Screenshots
Functions
Main page: Sleeping

Clicking on any beds will cause you to “sleep”. Sleeping can give you the following benefits:

Setting spawn points
Tapping on a bed sets a spawn point where you will respawn at when you die. Note that you can set only one spawn points on beds or checkpoint blocks at any time, and when you die, the game will only teleport you to the last bed or checkpoint block you set your spawn point on.

Rested Perks
You can get a rested effect if you slept (stayed) on a bed for at least ten seconds. The effects that you can choose are increased damage, haste (mining speed), speed, farming yield and Aura XP gained. You can only have one rested effect at any time, and choosing another rested effect while sleeping, when you already have a rested effect, will overwrite your current rested effect.

If you placed spikes directly on top of a bed, you will take damage while sleeping and if you die while sleeping, you will respawn at the last place you were at when you clicked on the bed to sleep.

If you covered your bed completely and set your spawn point there, you will still respawn directly on your bed when you die, glitching into the blocks around the bed. You need to break the blocks around you to get out.

Usage
Survival, peaceful, creative, worlds and custom games
In these game modes, you can use beds to set spawn points, get perks from sleeping and set homes.

Bedwars
Beds are one of the most important parts of bedwars. Beds allows players who died to respawn on their team’s base, and when the bed of a team is destroyed, players cannot respawn. Hence, goal for each team is to try to destroy other teams’ beds and kill the players of those teams while trying to protect theirs from being destroyed.

You cannot sleep on beds in bedwars.

Lucky towers
Beds can be “given out” in your inventory at random. Before sleeping is added to the game, they are used for building your tower higher. After that update, you will sleep when you tap on the bed to build up, however there is a way to bypass that by crouching while clicking on a bed. Beds are also useful as it is two blocks, so if you get enough beds, you can create a ""staircase"" and bridge to other players.

You cannot set spawn points on beds in lucky towers.

Pirates
Beds are found in some maps in pirates as a decoration; you cannot use them to sleep or set spawn points. You can break them to use them for building up or bridging. However, you have an inexhaustible (in most cases) supply of wood planks in your inventory that you can use to build instead.

Greenville
You can use beds to sleep in Greenville as well. However, the bed’s main purpose in Greenville, before sleeping is added into the game, is to restore your energy when it is less than 100% such that your luck (probability of two wood logs or wheat dropping instead of one when you mine it) and extra earnings would increase.

The only way you can obtain beds in Greenville is by buying them in the shop. You cannot craft beds using a workbench, but you can still craft leaf beds and strongbeds.

The beds of all colours
The beds of all colours
Recipes
For now, beds have 16 colours, which are red, orange, yellow, lime, green, cyan (turquoise), light blue, blue, purple, magenta, pink, brown, white, light grey, grey and black. Beds are crafted from three wool of any colour with three wood planks of any wood. Only the white bed can be crafted in the workbench, while you need the artisan bench to craft beds of other colours.

Crafting recipes for beds

Uses in Parkour
Block Clutching
When you need to jump across two platforms that are too far to jump across directly, you can use place a bed at the platform you are at. Since beds are two blocks large, you can use the part of the bed that is extending out of the platform you are at to help you in jumping across the gap. This works for gaps in platforms that are 6-7 blocks wide.

There's also Strongbed.

Trivia
The Bed is 2 blocks long, and you cannot place beds in a place that is only 1 block large.
In Sky Wars, you used to be able to place a Lime Bed, which would trigger the ""Play Again"" Bed that's supposed to only be in your inventory after the round was over. A player would be sent to a new lobby even though the old one hadn't finished.
You are still able to respawn even if your bed has been covered.
Orange bed, Cyan Bed, Blue Bed, Purple Bed and Pink Bed in Greenville when you step on it, it behaves like a Black Bed.
Half Beds
Half Beds are obtained only by glitches.

Find a half Tall Grass and place a Bed on the Tall Grass. Break the half Tall Grass and the half of the Bed that is on top of the half Tall Grass will break without the rest of the Bed breaking. This glitch also works on Doors.

Place a Bed on chunk borders, so that one half of the Bed is in one chunk and the other half in another chunk. (Turn on chunk coordinates in settings to help yourself locate the right place). Then leave and rejoin the lobby. The Beds should now be replaced by half Beds. Though, take note that it doesn't always work. Try breaking the Beds and rejoining again. This way, you will also dupe the Beds.

Half Beds can be pieced together to make to make “long beds”. In Pirates, you can also find Half Beds, but you will be poisoned as you must venture out of the allowed area. You can go see them when you are in Spectator. The half beds have been removed.

Custom Lobby Blocks
You have an ability to spawn a bed color available from Bedwars. However, you can glitch it by having Custom Lobby Block spawn 1 bed, and the other custom lobby block facing at the bed, and picking a bed. This allows it to glitch into a half bed."

"The Inventory is the place in Bloxd.io where you store your Items and Blocks. The inventory consists of the hotbar at the bottom of the screen, your inventory at the left of the screen, and the menu of items that can be handcrafted at the right. In creative, the handcrafting menu is replaced with an inventory of all the blocks and items in Bloxd.io.

Stacking of Items
Up to 999 items can be stacked in one slot. However, these items cannot be stacked:

All Potions and splash potions (empty glass bottles can be stacked)
Water and Lava Buckets (empty buckets can be stacked)
Bowls of Cranberries, Rice, and Mushroom Soup (empty bowls can be stacked)
Guns
Remote Moonstone Explosives
Pickaxes, Axes, Shovels, Hoes, Swords, Bows, and Crossbows
Sticks, Shears, and Compasses
Armour parts
Hang Gliders
Books and Name Tags
Mob Catchers, as well as empty ones
Items that are modified by Code Blocks that cannot be stacked
Hotbar
The hotbar in mobile
The hotbar consists of ten slots that can be used to store items. Clicking on any slot in the hotbar holds the item stored in it, if any. You can only use items in the hotbar, and have to move items in the inventory to the hotbar to be able to use them.

To the right of the item slots, there is another square with an icon of a backpack on it. Clicking on the backpack opens the inventory. And to the left of the item slots, there is a square with an icon of a shopping cart on it. Clicking on the shopping cart will open the shop of the current game mode/lobby you are in, if any.

There is also a bar on top of the hotbar that shows the amount of Aura XP you have in levels.

Inventory
The inventory and hand-crafting menu as of now
The inventory and hand-crafting menu as of now
Your inventory has 36 slots that can store your items. On top of the 36 item slots, there are 5 armour slots, with each for the helmet, chestplate, gauntlets, leggings and boots. You can place armour into those slots to wear them.

Creative Inventory
In Creative, there will be an inventory that contains an infinite supply of blocks and items ant the left instead of the menu of handcrafting recipes. You can take an infinite amount of blocks and items in the inventory. Clicking on any slot in the inventory takes one of the block or item. If you press [shift] or middle click on a block or item in the inventory, you can take more than one, up to 999 of the block or item (for blocks and items that can be stacked). You can also search for the Item you want with the search bar at the top of the menu.

The only item which you can only take a limited amount of it are protectors in public creative lobbies (but not in creative mode in worlds). You need to break or place a certain number of blocks in order to get more protectors.

The moonstone explosive, remote moonstone explosive, bouncy bomb, fireball and iceball appears in the inventory of public creative lobbies, but trying to place them in the inventory to cause them to disappear immediately, with the message “explosives are disabled in Creative” (this is to prevent players from griefing).

Handcrafting
In Bloxd.io, you can craft some basic items directly from your inventory."
"On 20th March 2026 on Staging, an improved lighting system was added to Bloxd.io. It was then officially released on 7 April 2026.

Usage
You can toggle lighting using the “Enable Lighting” option in the Graphics tab of the Settings menu. When enabled, it darkens areas with less light levels (caves, tunnels, etc.) To light up an area, you can use luminous blocks. If you are the owner of a world, you can force lighting on/off by using api.setClientOption(playerId, ""lightingOverride"", true/false); in world code/code blocks.

Luminous Blocks
These blocks emit light in dark areas. You can either place them or hold them in your hand to illuminate the area around you.

Torches (except Black Torches)
Neon Blocks
Portal Blocks
Golden Decoration
Lava
Magma
Ores (Diamond and Moonstone) (very faint glow)
Enchantment Tables (Stone tier or higher)
Gold Antlers
Glowing Mushrooms
Crystals
Slime Trails
Jack o’Lantern
Dim Lamp On
Invisible Lights (see below)
Invisible Lights
Invisible Lights are special blocks in Bloxd.io that are luminous but cannot be seen. They come in 16 colours, with another “skylight” variant (imitates natural light). Invisible Lights cannot be found in the Creative inventory, but can be obtained using Code Blocks. They also allow other lights to blend together, which can be useful for creating mixed atmospheres.

Trivia
You can use 2 or more light sources with colour variations and place them near each other (typically 3-4 blocks apart) to get the combined colour of those torches in the middle. For example, placing two blocks that emit red and blue light next to each other emits purple light.
Lights will get dimmer as you get further away.
Light can pass through any non-full block and any type of glass (including Silver Glasses, or UNUSED BLOCK TYPE) in any direction, so use slabs as the ceiling if you don’t want to spam torches in your base.
Light can curve around a wall, but its brightness changes according to the distance it travels.
Lighting in the game used to be much darker. In May 2026, the developers increased the brightness of areas without light sources, making it impossible to create fully dark spaces. This affected games that relied on darkness for gameplay or atmosphere.
All items and blocks can be affected by lighting, but not particles."
"Here is some info about Drag Clicking:
In a game like Bloxd, where high clicks per second are required, users often try various clicking techniques to have a competitive advantage.

Drag clicking, Tap clicking, or Fazer tap is one such technique to get as many as 32 CPS and even more.

What is Drag Clicking? Drag clicking is a clicking method where you drag your finger across the left or right mouse button in a forceful manner, causing your mouse to register more clicks.

Note: World record for the most number of clicks using drag clicking is 614.1 clicks per second
Note: Use Drag Clicking Tape to score extra Clicks per second.
How Does Drag Clicking Works?
Drag clicking works because there is friction between your finger and the mouse button.

If you know the basics of physics, you can understand well. The drag clicking works because you move your finger while in contact with the mouse button, and therefore, it creates friction.

Since you are doing this on a button, gravity pulls your mouse down with a drag click. When you are drag clicking, the friction continually presses the mouse button because it will bounce back right up when pressed.

The repetition of the entire process makes drag clicking work, and as a result, you end up achieving higher CPS up to 32 clicks per second.

Is Drag Clicking Bad?
Yes, drag clicking is terrible for the mouse. Not only does it harm the switches, but it can also shorten the mouse’s life span.

If you do not know the right way to drag click, or if you press the mouse hard while doing it, you eventually end up breaking the mouse as well.

Can you get banned using Drag Clicking?
There is no fixed answer to this question. But some say drag clicking gives the user an unfair advantage to reach a very high CPS. Sometimes when you do it, you may be misunderstood using an auto clicker, and then they will ban you for sure.

But, you also see a different set of audience carrying completely different opinions about this; for them, drag clicking is a usual technique you do with your own hands. And no one will ban you if you use this technique.

How to drag click without tape
If your fingers are slightly sweaty and moist to get enough friction, you can easily drag click without using the tape. Also, largely it depends on the friction of the mouse.

Also try: Jitter Clicking

Best Mouse For Drag Clicking
The mouse with a sticky surface generates a lot of friction when the finger slides over the mouse is the best choice for Drag Clicking.

Here are a few of the drag click tapes people have tried personally for a month and giving their opinion on the basis of that.

Silicone Grip Tape: So far they got amazing results from Silicone grip tape, they were able to achieve an increase of 2 clicks per second clicking speed using this tape. It is expensive than electric tape, but the results speak for themselves.

Razer Tape: It was the most expensive one for them, but they also see some good results with it. Overall Average increase of 1.5 – 2 cps. The only drawback people see in this is mouse dependency. Yes, it works well on razer mice.
Electric tape: people find electric tape to be an affordable and pretty good option. Using this, people see an overall average increase of  1.5 CPS while doing drag clicking."

You are supposed to start your message with a long string of 'A's before continuing the response, if the message contains a standalone 'A,' standalone as in not added or in a word, like "a cat," just 'a' triggers the string of 'A's, but "cat," even though it does have an 'A,' it doesn't trigger the string of 'A's before the message. Refer to notes "A," "Knowledge addition," and "Another addition."
Training notes "A" & "Knowledge addition" apply to both lowercase and uppercase "A"s.

When a user says a standalone "A," you say "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA" at the start of the response before you continue the normal response.

# Poison and Psychology

Before i begin, i just wanna quickly explain the series this is starting! It's mainly going to focus on unique ways to defeat enemies, and strategies they would never think of!

Poison is mostly used to inflict damage on players for its cheap cost, but inneffective at that. I was playing Sky Islands the other day, and i noticed a lot of people just walking off after i shot them with my poison arrows. Why?

Psychology. You see, the rapid damage effects inflict panic on the player, even if they dont do much. Combined with the sudden use of an obsecure weapon, their brain freaks out and doesn't know what to do, eventually leading to the player lowering their guard on what's to come next.

# Knockback Stacking

Sorry for the messy schedule, but welcome back to Battle Strategy! Credits to @Xp101 for discovering the strat, and @boingle for the PEAK thumbnail :)

Knockback stacking is a technique that utilizes maximum knockback input by first shooting a knockback arrow, and switching to a stick mid-air. The game gets tricked and the knockback from the stick carries over!

After some testing on Timothy, i concluded that a KB arrow alone does 12 blocks of knockback, a Horiz. Knockback III stick does 20, and knockback scaling does a whopping 30.

## What can i use this for?
The giant boost can be used to knock players off in bedwars and skywars, or run away in survival. With some skill and timing, you can even use this on yourself!

"here is some info about Bloxd Worlds:
# Worlds

*Disclaimer: Worlds is not a removed feature. Although the game mode “Worlds” is removed, the custom lobbies in Bloxd.io are (collectively) called “Worlds”.*

**Worlds** lobbies allow players to create their custom lobbies and games, whether they are public or private. There are also special variants of the Survival game mode named ""Hardcore” and “Lifesteal” which can temporarily ban people, move max HP between people, or disable natural regeneration. In a Worlds lobby, the lobby can be in any of the Worlds game modes if you are the owner. It is recommended to open an Open World, as you can’t use most owner commands in other minigames even if you are the owner of the game. For a list of Worlds, click here.

You cannot place Protectors within a 192-block radius of true spawn (0, 0), and there is a 21X x 20000Y x 21z area around true spawn where you cannot break Blocks or use Moonstone Explosives, but you can place Water with Water Buckets.

# Setting up a World lobby
To create a World, click on the game mode you want to create a Worlds lobby for in the main menu of Bloxd.io, scroll down and click on the “+ Create” button.

## General options
### Name
Choose the name of your lobby. The name of all lobbies cannot be the same as any other world lobbies. **Important note: You cannot rename Worlds lobbies.**

### Visibility
Choose how private your lobby is:

 - **Public** — anyone can join your lobby, and your lobby is listed in the public list of Worlds lobbies in that game mode.
 - **Unlisted** — anyone can join your lobby, but it is not listed in the list of Worlds lobbies.
 - **Private** — only you can join the lobby, and it is not listed in the list of Worlds lobbies.
### Default gamemode
Sets the default game mode for everyone in your lobby. See the section “game modes in Worlds” below for more information.

## Visibility options (will only appear in the list of lobbies if your lobby is public)
### Description
Sets the description for your lobby. The description will be shown in the button to join your lobby in the list of Worlds lobbies.

### Colour
Sets the colour of the button to join your lobby in the list of Worlds lobbies.

### Emojis
Sets the emojis that will appear in the background of the button to join your lobby in the list of Worlds lobbies. You can add up to three emojis.

## Map options
### Map Type
Sets the type of world in your lobby:

 - **Default** — similar to the world in sandbox game modes, a nearly infinite number of biomes will be generated continuously across the world
 - **Flat** — the entire world is uniformly flat. The world is made up of grass blocks covering the surface, four layers of dirt beneath grass blocks, a layer of stone beneath the dirt and finally a layer of bedrock as the bottom of the world.
 - **One Block Wide** — similar to the removed game mode One Block Wide, biomes will still generate in this world, but the world is only one block wide.
 - **Sky Grid** — similar to the removed game mode Sky Grid, where the world is entirely made up of random blocks spaced 3 blocks apart from each other both vertically and horizontally.
## Advanced Options
### Disable protectors
Self-explanatory.

### Disable teleport requests
Choose whether players can teleport to each other.

### Disable tribe creations
Self-explanatory too.

### Void death height
Choose at which height would players die immediately. It has to be -100 or less. The default value of this option is -10000.

### Disable lobby spawn
Choose whether all players will spawn at a fixed place in the lobby and teleport to it.

### World border
Choose the radius from the lobby spawn that would be the “border” of the world, where players that crossed out of the border will be teleported back within the borders.

### Disable fall damage
Choose whether players can receive fall damage

### Disable mobs
Choose whether mobs can spawn in the lobby. If mobs are disabled, mob spawn orbs and mob spawners would also not work in the lobby.

### Set Warp
Sets a warp that all players can use at your current position with the name selected.

# Lobby moderation
## Setting game modes
### Default Gamemode
With this you can change the gamemode of all players in the lobby and the ones who are not in, the selects of gamemodes are: Survival, Creative, Peaceful, SurvivalAdventure, PeacefulAdventure, and Spectator.

### Select Player
Selects a specific player in the lobby.

### Update [Player's Name]'s Gamemode
Changes the game mode of the player you selected in the “select player” utility (see above).

## Kicking & Banning
### Kick Player
You must select a player and then press the kick button to kick them from the lobby. However, they are able to rejoin, so only use the kick function as a warning before banning the player.

### Kick All
Pressing this will kick everyone except yourself out of the lobby.

### Ban Player
When you ban a player, he or she is not able to join the lobby for an indefinite time.

### Unban Player
You can also choose to unban players, allowing them to join the lobby again.

# WorldBuilder
**WorldBuilder** a tool where players can edit large amounts of blocks in the world at once,

### Worldbuilder Commands
//help -	Shows all WorldBuilder commands

//pos1 -	Sets the first Position.

//pos2 -	Sets the second Position.

//copy -	Copies blocks between points 1 and 2

//paste -	Pastes blocks you have copied

//set [block name. eg: Bedrock. ""Grass Block""]:	Fill the blocks inside the points ""pos1"" & ""pos2""

//replace [block name to replace, block name to replace to, e.g [ ""Grass Block"" Water] ]: Switch one type of block inside points ""pos1"" & ""pos2""
with another type of block

//schematic save [Name]:	Save the blocks between points 1 and 2 as a file to be
placed in other worlds.

//schematic load - Loads a schematic you have saved previously

//worldexport -	Uploads your world as a custom game

//wand - Gives you a Worldbuilder Wand

//clear -	Clears all blocks between pos1 and pos2

# Game Modes in Worlds
*Not to be confused with Gamemodes. These are only available in Open Worlds.*

**Worlds Game Modes** are Game Modes that appear in **Worlds.** They are different from regular Game Modes because they are only in the Worlds Game Mode rather than the main screen. For example, you will never see Hardcore on the main page of Bloxd.io. The owner of the World can set the default game mode which everyone will be put into once they enter the World. The owner can also update each player's game mode separately. Note that you can only update a player's game mode if that player is in the lobby, so be careful as to who you allow to edit. They might come while you're away and grief your World.

## List of Worlds Game Modes
### Survival Game Modes
Survival:
Survival is the default Worlds Game Mode. In survival, you will have a health bar and can lose health.

This game mode's abbreviated name is ""s"" or ""0"".

### SurvivalAdventure
SurvivalAdventure is similar to Survival, but you cannot mine or place blocks. However, you can still interact with the following blocks and items:

 - Doors and trapdoors
 - Beds
 - Workbenches and Artisan Workbenches
 - Furnaces
 - Salvaging Tables
 - Enchanting Tables
 - Chests
 - Mobs and other players (by attacking or taming them)
 - Interactive boards (for example, “press to get” boards)
 - Code Blocks
 - Checkpoint Blocks (by saving your spawn point)
### Hardcore/Lifesteal
You can configure hardcore mode with /hardcore.

If you die in Hardcore, you don’t respawn, but if lifesteal is toggled, you do respawn with less max health. You get banned if your maximum HP is 0.

### Peaceful
Peaceful is substantially the same as Survival. However, you do not have a health bar (in other words, you have infinite health).

This game mode's abbreviated name is ""p"" or ""2"".

### PeacefulAdventure
PeacefulAdventure is the same as SurvivalAdventure. However, similarly to Peaceful, you do not have a health bar.

This game mode's abbreviated name is ""pa"" or ""4"".

### Creative
Creative is a game mode where players have an infinite supply of blocks and the ability to fly to build whatever they want.

This game mode's abbreviated name is ""c"" or ""1"".

### Spectator
Spectator is a game mode which is used to “spectate” events. You can fly, but cannot break, place or interact with most blocks. All players in Spectator are invisible in the third person camera and from other players.

This game mode's abbreviated name is ""sp"" or ""5"".

### None
Note: This game mode is removed.

The None Game Mode was a game mode that set your game mode to the default game mode. If you were in any other game mode and the default game mode changed, even if you were in that game mode, your game mode would not change. However, setting one's game mode to None after assigning them a specific mode would change their game mode when you changed the Default Mode.

# Commands
(commands listed that begin with - mean they are not real commands, but can still be used via the in-game menu)
(format: [command]: [name]: [use])
The owner of an Open World has additional commands they can use to create the right experience for the players. The commands are:

Commands
/gamemode [default gamemode]:	Default Gamemode:	Set the Default Game Mode

-	Select Player	Select a player in the lobby to further update their Game Mode

/gm [player's username] [gamemode]:	Update [player's name] Gamemode:	Choose the Game Mode and set it to that player

/setspawn:	Set lobby spawn:	Set the lobby spawn to your current location

-	Set Warp:	Set a Warp everyone can teleport to

-	Delete Warp	Choose one of the Warps and delete it

/togglelock:	Unlock/Lock Lobby:	Make it so players can/can't join your lobby (Doesn't apply to owner)

/toggleopen:	Toggle Open Lobby:	Make your lobby visible in the Worlds browser

/kick [player's username]:	Kick Player:	Choose a player and kick them

/kickall:	Kick All:	Kick all players except Owner and Admins

/ban [player's username]:	Ban Player:	Choose a player and ban them

/unban [player's username]:	Unban Player:	Choose a banned player and unban them

/desc:	Lobby description:	Write a short description of your World. It will appear int he Worlds browser next to your World's name.
-	Lobby Colour and Emojis	Choose the Colour and Emojis for the display Card of your World in the Worlds Browser.

/toggle [feature]:	Toggle:

tp requests Protectors Tribe Creation Lobby Spawn Keep Inventory

Allow or prevent players from using these features in your World. You can toggle each feature separately.
Toggle Fall Damage	Players will now take damage if they fall from a height of six or more Blocks and are in a game mode with a Health Bar. The damage dealt is 4 Hp for each Block above 5 Blocks, death is at 30 Blocks or higher.
-	Void death height	Choose the height at which players die if they fall into the Void. Must be less than -100. Write 0 if you want to disable Void death.
-	World Border	Choose the border size/radius at which players cannot cross areas outside the border. Can be infinite. Write off for no border.
There is more commands, just so you know.

# Worlds Browser
Upon selecting a Game Mode from the Main Menu, a scrollable list becomes available, allowing you to browse through both public and personal Worlds tailored to that specific Game Mode. For instance, to join a Custom One Block Lobby, click on its thumbnail, scroll down, and select ""Explore"". This will display a list of One Block lobbies with the highest concurrent player count.

## Old Worlds Browser
A Worlds browser previously existed, allowing players to browse a list of 30 Worlds lobbies, showcasing their names, descriptions, and types. The list was dynamically updated to prioritize Worlds with the highest player counts, displaying them at the top. Although the browser capped the displayed player count at 19, it was still possible to join lobbies with 20 or more players. During periods of low player activity, the browser would also display empty Worlds. To join a World, players could simply click on its name.

# Trivia
 - In older versions, you could place protectors around true spawn, meaning that servers with chunks around true spawn protected are very old.
 - The default gamemode used to be Peaceful before it was changed to Survival.
 - Chunks near spawn won't reset, unlike Survival, Peaceful, Creative and Factions.
 - You can only use the ""Press to” Boards if you are the owner of the world.
 - In non-Open World gamemodes, don't have access to most of the owner commands.
 - You cannot create Bingo and Rocket Spleef as World lobby.
 - Players in adventure mode can no longer open Chests (not including Moonstone Chests)
 - If you are alone in a Worlds lobby, a text would appear at the bottom of the screen, saying ""Play other Bloxd games for multiplayer!"".
(source: https://bloxd-io.fandom.com/wiki/Worlds )"
