"here is some Building tips for Bloxd.io:
This really helps if u want to build a really good base/ help someone in building servers like cities

Simple tips:

Detail your builds with same colour, but different blocks

If u don't like detailing, Morden builds is better for you. Just use some repetitive patterns then you will have your own costom build

If your build is just too clean, make some broken stuff like using barkless maple log on maple planks to make it look like scraped off, or, you can add some plants into the build.

detailing also contains depth, making depth is will make your build look a lot better. make it by using slabs or block placing one block outwards, making the build look layered.

Take some inspirations, if you don't have idea what kinda theme u want to build, inspirations helps pretty well.

If you really like building, you should go to creative words and not survival words. (except you want a base)

Advanced tips:

Do not overdetail something. It will make it look bad

Different places needs different blocks to detail

Here are the most common ones:

Pathway: Dirt, Tilled Soil, Messy dirt, Rocky dirt, Grass, Messy stone, Grass slab, Messy Stone Slab

Stone Pathway: Smooth Stone, Stone, Engraved Stone, Messy Stone, Stone Slab, Messy Stone Slab.

Stone Piles: Stone, Messy Stone, Mossy Stone, Vines, Mossy Stone Slabs, Messy Stone Slabs

-Grass fields: Grass slab, Dirt, Wheat, Fully Grown Rice, Cranberries, Flowers

3. Remember, Some builds need detail but some don't. Like mansion builds keep it simple is already good.

4. Well I have to say that you have to quit pvp if you are one that is sick of pvp. because pvp spends time, and also could make u distracted while building.
(Source: https://www.reddit.com/r/bloxd/comments/1f5fnbs/how_to_be_a_good_builder_in_bloxd/ )"

"here are some facts about Bloxd.io tribes:
Overview
Tribes
FactionsTribes Icon
Faction/Tribe Icon
Tribes in Creative and Worlds are clans that work together to achieve things. 2 Iron Bars are required to create a tribe, even if you are the world owner or a lobby. Your name and everyone else in your Tribe’s name will all turn green. Pressing ""Tab"" while in the chat will turn it into the Tribe chat where you can talk privately. You will have the word [Tribe ] next to your name and your message will be green. A Tribe home can also be made and everyone in your Tribe can teleport to it. There can be 20-30 players in a Tribe before it gets full and no more players can join.

Usage
If you are the manager of the tribe, a new tab in your utilities menu will pop up called ""Manage Faction"" or ""Manage Tribe"". Below is a list of things you can do:

Name of command	Function
Invite player	Invite a player to your tribe. A notification will pop up like a tprequest notification. Press Y to accept and X to decline, or click Yellow ""Y"" or Red ""X"" on the right hand side on you screen if you are on mobile.  
Remove player	Remove a player from your Tribe
Enable/Disable Members PvP	Enable PvP in your Tribe
Set Faction home	Set your faction home. All players in your faction can teleport to your home.
Disband Faction	Disband your Faction. All players cannot access the faction home OR talk privately.
Toggle Faction Chat	Turn on faction chat - this command is now removed because in the chat you press tab to change.
Tribe Protector
Tribe Protector1
Information
Maximum Stack
999


Tribe Protector
It is similar and works like a regular Protector, but all members of the tribe can use and edit the chunks protected by the protector. It can be obtained if you are the tribe leader, by pressing B on PCs or the shopping trolley/cart symbol at the bottom left corner on mobile. Then, press Manage Faction/Tribe , and you can buy tribe protectors in that menu for 2 Moonstone and 1 stone of any kind.

Trivia
Tribes used to be called Factions.
The Tribe Protector led to a glitch that deleted protectors.
In the Factions, Tribes are called Factions."
"here is some info about Protectors in Bloxd.io:
Protector
Talk
5
Save
Edit
Protector
Protectoresdasd
Information
Maximum Stack
999
Best Tool to Mine
Pickaxe
Protectors are blocks in Bloxd.io that protect a chunk (most often, with your builds inside), from being tampered with by other players. Players cannot interact with blocks in protected chunks of other players except for utility blocks (e.g. workbenches and artisan benches), moonstone chests, doors and trapdoors.

9.1	Protector
9.2	Faction Protector
Appearance
The Protector's appearance is characterized by a gray exterior, adorned with a cyan aura at its center, and intricate engravings that surround it. A prominent blue dot is also visible, symbolizing the Diamonds required for its crafting. Its design has undergone a revision, having previously resembled Diamond Ore without shadows, until a update in late 2022 introduced its current aesthetic.

Obtaining
Crafting in Survival/Peaceful (Via Workbench)
Name	Crafting recipe
Protector	2 Diamonds + 1 Stone
Creative
In Creative, you can get protectors by breaking or placing a certain amount of blocks.

Protector	Number of Blocks placed/broken to get the protector
1st	200
2nd	600
3rd	1000
4th	1800
5th	2600
6th	4200
7th	5800
8th	9000
Plots
In Plots, you can use as many protectors as you want as a decoration; you do not need to mine or place a certain number of blocks to get a protector, as your plot is always protected from other players (except those that you allowed to edit your plots).

Other game modes
Greenville
It used to be possible to get Protectors in Greenville with a glitch. Protectors does not protect chunks in Greenville, as the players’ houses in Greenville are protected at all times from anyone else (except those that are selected in the house config menu that can edit the houses).

Doodle Cube
In Doodle Cube, no players can edit your builds at any time. Hence, you can use as many protects as you want in your builds in doodle cube.

Protector Glitch (Fixed)
You used to be able to get infinite number of protectors by going to the recipe and shift clicking craft (you would need lots of diamonds to get more). This glitch has been fixed by the developers and you can't do it anymore.

Editing Protected Chunks
You can select which players can edit the blocks in your protected chunk. However, be careful with who to choose that can edit your chunk as they can destroy your builds.

Protector Disappearing
All the protectors of a player will disappear if they are offline from a lobby for 30 days or longer to prevent players from hogging chunks in the world for too long. It has been rumored that this feature has been removed.

It is unknown whether the developers can disable protectors, but this is likely the case for them to remove inappropriate builds quickly.

Tribe Protector
Tribe Protector
Tribe Protector1
Information
Maximum Stack
1
Best Tool to Mine
Pickaxe


Tribe protectors are similar to Protectors, but all members of the Tribe can edit blocks in the protected chunk. They can only be obtained when you are a leader of a tribe (created a tribe). Each tribe protector is created from 2 Moonstone and 1 Stone of any kind in the “manage tribe” menu in the b-menu of the game mode. If you disband a Tribe, any Tribe Protectors you have placed will disappear.

Faction Protector
Faction Protector
Faction Protector1
Information
Maximum Stack
999
Best Tool to Mine
Pickaxe

A Faction Protector was similar and worked like a regular Tribe Protector, but all members of a Faction can use and edit it. It existed only in Factions. It could not be crafted, only obtainable in Factions for 100 Gold Coins, on the condition that you are the Faction Owner. The maximum number of Faction Protectors a Faction could have depended on the amount of power it had. If Factions lost power, they could have more Faction Protectors than were allowed by their power. This could make a Faction Vulnerable, allowing others to raid them and break one of their Faction Protectors by pressing F in one of the Faction's protected chunk. This was the only way to break other Faction Protectors.

Placing Protectors
Protectors does not protect blocks in a certain radius around it, but instead the blocks in the chunk (a 32 x 32 x 32 cube) it is in, regardless of which position is the protector in the chunk. Turn on chunk coordinates (from the settings) to track which chunk you are in to plan where to place your protectors.

Commands
There is a command, /protectors, that tells you where your Protectors are. Type it into chat and you will get a list of your placed Protectors and their coordinates. In Creative, you will also get info about how many Protectors you have unlocked.

Trivia
Protector
At some point in March, Arthur added the Tribe Protector for Tribes such that players in a tribe can easily have access to the tribe owner’s protected chunks. But in the process of adding that, Arthur accidentally reset or deleted all of the Protectors. Many bases got looted, grieved, and even protected by other players. This made a lot of players angry because they lost tons of Items or progress because of that. So, Arthur “refunded” them in the form of protectors, Blocks of Moonstone, and Blocks of Diamond depending on how long the player has been playing on the server.4 protectors touching each other
4 protectors touching each other

Protectors are gray with a cyan aura—representing the Diamonds used in crafting them—in the middle and engravings surrounding it. But before late 2022, they used to look like a Diamond Ore without the shadow, and no texturing or disorientation.
You can place 4 Protectors next to each other by placing them in the chunk borders of four chunks, and you cannot go further since protectors protect 32x96x32 area.
Protectors used to be limited by crafting. This means you could only craft 8 Protectors and their price also increased with each new Protector you crafted. You could only craft a new Protector if you lost one of the old ones. Later, Arthur changed it so you can now craft unlimited Protectors all for the same price. You can still only have 8 placed Protectors at once however.
Protector cannot be collected in Greenville, it is only a decorative block.
Faction Protector
The red and yellow texture of this type of protector only shows if you are using the default texture pack. Otherwise, it looks the same as a Tribe Protector. At least it used to though, as one texture pack creator, L16_F51620, went and tried to modify the protector textures. The last texture of the texture pack used to be a faction protector at the time, so L16 went and figured out how to add them. Also, the developers forgot to actually update the default texture pack link, which is why he told everyone how to add faction protector textures."
"Here are some strategies cover multiple gamemode in Bloxd:
Open World
Tutorial
A Stone Pickaxe; important tool for beginners
A Stone Pickaxe; important tool for beginners

Start by punching a Tree. It doesn't matter what type of Tree you punch. If you want a neat world, then you should break the Leaves as well. You should also break the Leaves if you are in a survival lobby for Food. The Wood you get from Trees can be used to craft basic Tools. The main Tool you want to craft is a Wooden Pickaxe. With this basic Pickaxe you can get Stone. Stone Tools are better than Wood and can mine Ores like Coal, Iron, and Gold. This should help you get better gear.

Organizing items
You can organize the items you have to put them in chests in these categories:

food
bowls of rice/cranberries (they cannot be stacked)
potions (ditto)
splash potions (ditto)
seeds of crops and plants
weapons and tools
armor
empty buckets/bowls/glass bottles
decorative blocks
miscellaneous
Survival
The biggest threat in survival in Bloxd.io is hostile players, and the only way you can survive in there is to be yet another one of them.

Before you are strong enough to defeat other players, do not go anywhere near the spawn in the lobby. Other players are often there to kill new players.
Start with wood tools and armor, then progress to stone, iron, gold, and finally diamond ones.
Do not jump into caves to mine for ores. Caves in Bloxd.io are often massive and difficult to navigate. Instead, dig your own tunnels to find ores.
During the times when you are free, collect food, the seeds of plants that give you food, tree saplings, wood, and other blocks. Use the seeds and tree saplings to farm crops and trees.
Try to find loot chests in ruins. If the chest is emitting particle effects, then the chest has not been opened before. The colour of the effects determines the enchantment tier the tools or armor the chest contains are enchanted with.
If you want to skybase (build your house above the vertical rendering distance from the ground), ensure that you bring enough blocks and ladders to reach the desired height. You need ladders to climb up to the height to build your house before it is complete. When you have completed building your house, set a home there and do not leave the tower you used to reach the height where the house is, as other players might use that tower to find your house.
Use protectors to protect your house from being destroyed or looted.
Always set a home that you can teleport to in case you get lost while collecting resources.
Only start to kill players when you have a full set of diamond armour and weapons.
Bedwars
How to win:
Play with a friend, chat with them throughout the game. It's better to find an active player who is not a bot who would leave the second the game started.
Agree which of you goes to the center or to fight, and the other protects the Bed (depending on which of you is more active in the game)
As soon as the game starts, accumulate at least 32 Coal (depending on how far away the center is) and buy wool for all the Coal (64) and immediately go to the nearest island with a Moonstone generator and from there build up to the center. If you have Iron at the beginning of the game, then put it in one of the Chests (depending on who you are playing with). You need to do all this very quickly.
Waiting and not attacking immediately usually isn't a good idea. Dying isn't a big problem as long as you still have your Bed. A kamikaze in which you lose some Wool and a Sword is great if you manage to break an enemy Bed.
If you are the last team and you can't find the last remaining person because he is hiding, follow these steps.(Warning: This may be regarded as cheating. It's not recommended unless the hiding player shows no signs of planning to attack you.)
Log on to an alt account.
Join the Bedwars lobby you are currently in. This can be found in the top left corner.
You will be a spectator and have flying. Locate the hiding player. Please note that this could take a long time.
Make sure to go back to your main account and shake your mouse quite often. Otherwise, you will time out and be disconnected, forcing you to leave the game and causing you to lose the game.
Once you locate the hiding player, kill him.
There is a way to hide so that no one can find you unless they use the tip above. Also, this is not recommended, as this is unfair play.
Start the game by getting Wool from the generator.
Head to the back of the team base.
Fall off and place the Block downward.
Repeat the first three steps multiple times.
Repeat the first two steps one more time.
Place a Block on the side of the downward pillar as far down as possible, just above the void death height.
Jump onto the Block; you should be one Block from hitting the ceiling with your head.
Place Blocks while traveling until you are directly below the base.
Destroy the previously placed Blocks except the pillar and the Blocks below the base.
Stand on the singular Block.
Build a base around you.
Wait.
Win due to the other player giving up!
If you are playing on PC or Mac through a browser, it might be annoying to crouch because if you try to crouch and move forward, a message will pop up about how CTRL+W deletes tabs. To fix this, simply click ""Go full screen"" or the F11 key, or use C to crouch.
If you have the ability, wait until there's no one at the enemies' base before you break the Bed. Try to be sneaky.
Before attacking other bases, if they have strong Bed defenses, you should prepare yourself with a Fireball, 3 Moonstone Explosive, and a Stick. After breaking their Bed, try hitting the players with the Stick if they are next to the edge.
You can also rush with 3 or even 4 if your sweaty or good. You need to think tactically for this. It's crucial to be either a good PvPer or parkourist.
If you fall off and died in early game, you could quickly see others' bridging progresses. by looking up while falling. You can upper your render distance for better sight, although it may get a bit laggy.
CubeWarfare
Overview
The main issue of this game is that you lose all points upon death, while many griefers love griefing you on towers with a sniper (TAR-21 or AWP). Taking cover will not work nowadays as you could see nametags through walls. Do your best at running around, trying to get kills. There is never the ""best"" Gun. You should find the best Gun that YOU could use.

Choosing your BEST Gun
Try every Gun. Unlock all Guns. Most people goes best with MP40 or a Double Barrel, but that may not be you. Quick running Guns such as M1911 could be extremely strong as well, so try all of them!

Avoiding Griefers
Griefers snipe you on sky bases. Try build you own on a further location when you have enough Blocks. When it gets invaded, abandon that. It is really hard to reclaim and get back up once you've fallen. Also, if you prefer killing on the ground then do take cover under roofs sometimes to avoid sky snipers. Buy powerups at a safe spot as this is your weak point.

Pickaxe
Use a Pickaxe for melee fights. A Pickaxe is crucial if you get attacked while reloading or if you run out of ammo mid-fight.

Pirates
Kits
Try out all the Kits and choose the one that fits you most. Kits of rarity Legendary aren't always the best! Think about what you're good at and choose the Kit that is specialized for that.

Jumping
Something you will really need in Pirates are jumping skills. Due to a lot of Explosives, the ships and islands get a lot of holes through which you can fall and die. Knowing which jumps you can do and actually doing them is crucial for surviving.

Protecting spawns
If an enemy places a Moonstone Explosive on one of your spawns, shoot it with a Fireball or splash knockback potion. This will hit the explosive and shoot it away. It doesn't matter if you kill yourself or your team mates with this as long as the spawn is still there! ( Since you can respawn )

Sky Wars
Kits
As with Pirates, choose the Kit that fits you most and not the rarest one in your collection. Some Kits could be considered scams or jokes regardless of their rarity (like Guardian).

Rushing
A strategy is to rush to the middle where the best loot can be found. Equip yourself and wait till just a few players remain. Then try to kill them.This strategy can give you more wins but less kills.

Killing
The other strategy is in killing as many players as possible. You don't even need to go to the middle island in all maps. The problem is that you will be easily killed by others who went to the middle and have much better loot than you. This strategy yields less wins but more kills and more Coins.

Infection
Guns
It isn’t necessarily useful to buy every gun in the game as it costs a lot of extraction points, which can be used to unlock maps instead.

Teaming
The main strategy in Infection is being a good team. One lone player needs to be extremely good to make any progress. This applies to Humans and Zombies.

Farming
The easiest way for Humans to win is by farming Zombie kills in a corridor. All maps have at least one good farming corridor near the start of the map. For optimal farming you need about half Humans and half Zombies. Humans stand at one end of the corridor and shoot the Zombies coming into the corridor at the other end. Humans are (almost) invincible at this. You will collect thousands of points in a matter of minutes. After everyone has a lot of points, just run. The Zombies won't catch up with you.

Bloxd Hop
Speedrunning maps without boosts
An important factor in how long it takes for you to finish a map is how many times you fell of platforms and respawned, as falling into the void and starting again from the last checkpoint both wastes time. Hence, try to train yourself to reduce the times you respawn by playing more Bloxd Hop. Also, you can skip some platforms in some maps. You can figure out which platforms you can skip and remember that the next times you play Bloxd Hop to reduce time.

Trying to get as many bunny hops as possible is not recommended, as it is difficult to activate a bunny hop. Also, you will get a speed boost from bunny hops; however, that is not enough to jump across multiple platforms, and you can overshoot platforms and fall into the void easily with the speed boost.

Getting as much gold as possible
Not much strategies is required to earn gold faster, as jumping only every checkpoint will give you some gold, and you can simply play Bloxd Hop for longer periods of time to get more gold. Also, you can try to complete the tasks. The tasks will give you relatively more gold than saving checkpoints. Additionally, the list of tasks will be continuously updated when you finish tasks, making the tasks as a non-exhaustible source of gold.

There is an option to watch an advertisement to get 30 gold in the “rewards” section of the shop. This is not recommended, as you can get more gold by saving checkpoints for the time it takes for the advertisement to play. This does not work if you bought the Super Rank as all ads are disabled.

Speedrunning maps with boosts
Earn a lot of gold and buy many speed and jump boosts. The increase in speed and/or height you jump can be stacked when you buy multiple speed and/or jump boosts. (For example, buying 3 jump boosts will make you jump 3 times as high)

If you have enough speed and jump boosts, you can “fly” to the end of the parkour with one jump. If the timer is running out before you are satisfied with the time it takes for you to complete the map, buy the “reset timer” option in the shop to gain more time to “save” your speed and jump boosts. All boosts are cleared when you join a different lobby.

Try finding secrets!
There are a lot of places in some maps outside the parkour which can save you lots of time, also called an ""Developer secret"" by some people. This video shows one of them.

Evil Tower
Skill
A bit more of skill is needed to play Evil Tower. The maps are all different, but each separate floor is always the same. Learn each floor and you'll soon be able to complete the maps! Try practicing Bloxd Hop for a bit before starting with Evil Tower.

Avoiding Red Clay and not falling
If you're not trying to speedrun but just want to reach the top, try crouching at the edges of Blocks and before Clay. You can also always use Boosts if you have enough money. Keep in mind that some Boosts , especially Speed, can mess you up if you're already very used to the usual jumping.

Beware of Messy Stone
If you stand on Messy Stone, it will break after 1 second, so don't wait there for so long!

Tower generation could mess up sometimes
Sometimes it can happen that some blocks are in the way of a staircase, meaning that if you don't have enough Gold to buy an boost or if nobody dos a Server Boost, you won't be able to progress any further. If you do have enough Gold, try to buy Double Jump and continue like normal.

One Block
How to mine faster?
Seeing ""compare number of mined blocks"" is a problem in this game mode and these are the fastest ways to mine really fast.

Turn off chat to avoid being disturbed
Sub account required for faster mining
Use the right tools for the right block
Avoid AFK
Once you reach the Moon phase (the final phase) of the game mode, choose the easy phases (Plains, Volcano or Underground), avoid phases with wood or using a lot of tools or too many chests (Plenty, Desolation, Dungeon).
If you still have not made it to the portal, do not go. When you get bored after mining blocks on your One Block, you will get distracted and go to other's worlds instead of mining your own one block.
2nd Method (Only works with PC and not with Mobile)
Put your Pickaxe, Axe, Shovel and Hoe on the first 4 hotbars
Left-Click to start mining
Continuously press 1, 2, 3 and 4 number Keys
This allows you to quickly switch to other Tools while mining so that you can use the tight tools for the right block
This trick works best with Diamond Tools so that you can mine fast
Using this with the Rainbow Phase works best as there are only 2 types of tools needed and they can be mined fast
You can also use Moonstone Tools but take note that even though you will mine faster, the Moonstone Pickaxe will give you the same block when mining so it may be a bit of a hassle re-mining the blocks to get Coal, Diamonds, Moonstone or Messy Stone
Bridging
Bridging is building improvised bridges to get to other places in the map. This is especially important in Bedwars, Skywars and Pirates, that has huge gaps between places in the map that is too wide for you jump over to.

Ensure you have a reasonable number of blocks for you to get to your destination.
If you have a lot of blocks, try to build up or build a tall staircase before you bridge, which makes it difficult for other players to shoot arrows or projectiles accurately at you, while you are on the bridge.
Crouch or use the third-person camera if you are inexperienced at bridging.
Move to the edge of the block you want to bridge from.
Click on the side of that block to place another block, move backwards to the edge of the block you replaced and repeat until you are at your destination.
Factions
Note: this game mode has been removed. The strategies remain for archival purposes.

When playing faction mode, your number one goal is try to achieve a strong economy, and try to use one of the following method to farm materials to sell: mine at y=-86 to try to find diamonds and moonstones; Create a plot filled with saplings for lumbering; farm melons. These are the fast strategies to get a lot of gold.
NEVER let anyone teleport to you unless you have every diamond tools and is good in PVP or you know that's your friend. Otherwise, they will just kill you within their 10s immunity and take your stuff.
Building a base is also important. Make it very secret or get a protector.
Joining/creating factions are a much more minor thing for you if you are a party mode player(non-professional player). Just create a faction, get the protector, and throw a random base somewhere.
Always make your trade fast in Airship and crouch in it to hide your name tag, because players often hide there to kill others.
A really effective strategy in game is to warp to a market created by others. Check to see that no one of that faction is online, start a raid by pressing F, quickly break the chests containing valueable things (strength II potions, diamonds, gold, moonstone, etc.), end the raid, and go back to the market!
Base building
when building a base (especially in faction), consider the following:
first, your base's location should be ralatively hidden and far away from the spawn others and the 8 begginer camps in factions. Depending on your style, you have three types of good bases to choose:
First, underground bases. The entrance should be as hidden as possible. For example find a river, go to the botton, and then pick a corner to dig down and build your base beneath.
Note that never build your base too close to the bedrock layer, as people branch mining might accidentally run into it.
Also, make sure your there is a layer of air between your base and the outer stones and use something like coal ores and iron ores to cover it so that people using x-ray texture packs packs wouldn't suspect a base there.
Second, sky base. The sky base should be at least 130 blocks high so that even if players set their render distance to the highest, they still cant see your base without pillaring up.
Third, on land bases. These bases are prone to raids in faction mode, but with proper defense and enough player in your faction, it is not a problem. Make sure there is an underground/sky section in your base though, just so that you still have somewhere to hide and store valuable things."
"Some info about the gamemode Pirates (source: https://bloxd-io.fandom.com/wiki/Pirates):
Pirates is a gamemode that was introduced on December 6, 2023. Its primary objective is to destroy the opposing team's spawn blocks, followed by eliminating all players from the enemy team(s). The game mode was removed just 16 hours after its debut, preventing most players from experiencing its initial version. However, it has since been reintroduced later on bloxd.io as a permanent gamemode, as it won the vote against Survival Royale.

This gamemode was developed by DirtyFleaSack, a member of the Bloxd.io development team.

Tips (source: https://www.reddit.com/r/bloxd/comments/1j32vz6/most_important_pirates_tip_i_will_ever_give_some/ ):
Here is the important tip: Always get spawns before kills. There are no exceptions. A kill before all the spawns are broken is a kill wasted, and you just wasted time killing someone when you could have gotten a spawn. If someone is guarding a spawn, luring them away is more effective.

Other tips:

- Never fire cannons, since they are useless and cannot get spawns. They make it harder for those who are actually getting spawns

- Never build walls around spawns, as it just makes it harder to get out and easier to kill more people with 1 TNT

- Know the speedrunning routes. They make it faster to get spawns.

- Know the speedrunning routes for 2 players, so you do not intersect another speedrunner's path (if there is one) and you get spawns faster

- Wood axe is faster than TNT, so use them unless there are a lot of people around a spawn, or it is walled"
Emotes in Bloxd: There are new emotes in Bloxd, released in early May. By pressing the Z key, an emotes wheel will be shown, and you can pick from  the following emotes: Wave, Clap, Point, Shrug, Cheer, and Sit.
remember that when players talk about emotes in general, refer to your emote info! if they ask about emotes in code, make sure that, if u have enough info for what the player wants, that you can generate code using api.animateEntity, but for emotes in general, refer to your training about emotes!

"ok, here are some pvp tips you can use to help players in Bloxd.io. remember bloxd pvp is different than minecraft.:
Practise aiming
Aiming is essential as this ensures you can hit players efficiently. You can use Sword Fight or player-vs-bot games to improve your aim. If your aim improves it make sure you actually hit players and this can evolve into combos.

Critical hits
Critical hits are essential for advanced players. If you can find a 2-block space you can spam critical hits. However, Strafing around them then criting them out is also great. When running to your opponent you should always start with a critical hit. Critical hits are hard to perform for mobile players, so you should always strafe around them before hitting them.

Combos
Combos are a PvP technique used to continuously and quickly rack damage.

Combo types
There are 2 types of combos, which are ground and air combos. Ground combos are easier to perform but it is easier to break the combo. Air combos are extremely hard to perform but if done properly impossible to get out of due to Pythagoras Theorem.

How to perform combos
Ground combos are performed by repeatedly hitting players quickly while strafing making sure they are getting applied knockback. Spacing is key make sure you press s to make sure that they cannot hit you back. Air combos require you to knock them high in the air using critical hits then hitting them in the air. Due to Pythagoras theorem they will not be able to hit you back. If you get them in this position they will lose for sure because they will not be able to escape your combo.

Strafing
Strafing is a technique in PvP where you move around constantly while fighting with another player to avoid attacks.

How to strafe
You can strafe by moving left and right quickly, or even to the back of the player you are fighting with.

What does strafing do?
It makes it harder for enemies to hit you, and if you do it unpredictably with jumps, you can force players to turn around, making it extremely hard for players to hit you.

Strafing variants
Normal strafing
This Movement technique is where you move to the left and right by pressing A,D randomly this easiest but least confusing.

Crabwalk
This movement technique is where you walk to the left then forward of an opponent then to the right then fowardmaking it harder for opponents to hit you and usually confuses them less useful against experienced players.

Circle
This movement technique is when you walk in circles around your opponent switching directions every 2 or 3 rotations. It will be harder for your opponents to hit you then with Crabwalk but also harder to perform and also harder to aim.

Butterfly
This movement technique is when you turn 180 degrees after every 2 to 3 hits. The is also the hardest variant to perform and it is the most confusing.

Mixed
Combining all 3 technique makes a very good and confusing PVP Movement technique. Every few seconds, switch between the 3 and it will become extremely hard for the opponent to hit you combined with jumping this is the best possible technique.

Using blocks
You can use blocks to efficiently to block and shield yourself in PvP. Especially in games like Skywars you can run and block their way. You can also make shields by placing 2 blocks rapidly on top of each other you can block attacks. This is especially useful for blocking arrows when getting shot at. Blocks can also be used in games like Bedwars to box yourself in ( breaking bed ). Furthermore, it can be used in structures to block off doorways making it easier to run in games such as Skywars.

Types of PvP
Organized- PvP with both sides’ consent and is used for friendly purposes, like practicing skills.

Semi-organized- PvP where one side invites the other to engage in PvP with them. Can be hostile.

Unorganized- PvP without any sides’ consent and is often unexpected. This is always hostile.

PvP gear
Swords-your standard damage dealer

Clubs-extra knockback

Daggers-Lower damage but does more every slash

Maces-More damage the faster you go

Spears-Like the Minecraft spear propels you forward if used with the mace it can be effective hot bar switching

Armour-take less damage

Potions-gain buffs and gives others debuffs

Food-heal and buffs

net-a web to trap people

Moonstone Explosive-useful for running away and for dealing damage when the enemy is trapped

Moonstone Orb-useful for running away

how to use potions effectively
Potions give you Effects are essential in PvP and can give you the upper hand. Here is a list of some Potions and how to use them.

Strength- extra damage useful if your aim is bad.

Defence- You take less damage every hit. This can even out the damage between you and your opponent if you are outgeared.

Speed- If it is unorganized PvP then you can use speed potions to run.

Jump boost- Makes your strafing even better. Good for landing crits and also helps with running.

Double jump-Like Jump boost but better used specifically when you want to backstab.

Knockback- When you need to run or infiltrate a protected base, just drink it or splash at your feet

Other PvP items
Stick-increased knockback useful for games like Skywars

Mangoes/knockback pots -Good for running

PvP gamemodes
Bedwars

Skywars

Pirates

Clutch

1v1 Fights

Cube Warfare

Bedwars tips
Practise Bridging the faster you bridge the better

PvP is essential here with you having to kill players and break the bed.

A unique part of Bedwars shared with Skywars is that there are bridge fights

Knockback matters immensely on bridge fights and island fights buy a stick if they are better any outgear you use it."

"actually, the GitHub documentation provides an even more detailed way to make particle effects in bloxd! here is what it says:
# Particles

These are the strings you can give to functions that take a particle effect `texture` as input:

`bubble`
`critical_hit`
`drift`
`effect_5`
`generic_2`
`glint`
`heart`
`scary_face`
`soul_0`
`square_particle`
`z-particle`

Here's the code for an example particle effect:

```ts
let [x, y, z] = thisPos
y += 1
api.playParticleEffect({
    dir1: [-1, -1, -1],
    dir2: [1, 1, 1],
    pos1: [x, y, z],
    pos2: [x + 1, y + 1, z + 1],
    texture: ""bubble"",
    minLifeTime: 0.2,
    maxLifeTime: 0.6,
    minEmitPower: 2,
    maxEmitPower: 2,
    minSize: 0.25,
    maxSize: 0.35,
    manualEmitCount: 20,
    gravity: [0, -10, 0],
    colorGradients: [
        {
            timeFraction: 0,
            minColor: [60, 60, 150, 1],
            maxColor: [200, 200, 255, 1],
        },
    ],
    velocityGradients: [
        {
            timeFraction: 0,
            factor: 1,
            factor2: 1,
        },
    ],
    blendMode: 1,
})
```

You can also use a `presetId` instead to use a pre-defined particle effect, to replicate effects we use in-engine.
Here is the code for an example of using a presetId:

```ts
let [x, y, z] = thisPos
y += 1
api.playParticleEffect({
    presetId: ""aura"",
    pos1: [x, y, z],
    pos2: [x + 1, y + 1, z + 1],
})
```

"also, here is the code for making a particle effects in Bloxd.io using code!

let effectName = ""bubble""
let [x, y, z] = [thisPos[0], thisPos[1]+1, thisPos[2]]
api.playParticleEffect({
    dir1: [-1, -1, -1],
    dir2: [1, 1, 1],
    pos1: [x, y, z],
    pos2: [x + 1, y + 1, z + 1],
    texture: effectName,
    minLifeTime: 0.2,
    maxLifeTime: 0.6,
    minEmitPower: 2,
    maxEmitPower: 2,
    minSize: 0.25,
    maxSize: 0.35,
    manualEmitCount: 20,
    gravity: [0, -10, 0],
    colorGradients: [
        {
            timeFraction: 0,
            minColor: [60, 60, 150, 1],
            maxColor: [200, 200, 255, 1],
        },
    ],
    velocityGradients: [
        {
            timeFraction: 0,
            factor: 1,
            factor2: 1,
        },
    ],
  blendMode: 1,
})

You can replace ""bubble"" with other textures like: bubble, critical_hit, drift, effect_5, generic_2, glint, heart, soul_0, square_particle"

// =========================
// CREATE SHOPS ON JOIN
// =========================

onPlayerJoin = (pId) => {

    // BUY SHOP
    for (const item of buyItems) {
        api.createShopItemForPlayer(pId, ""Buy Shop"", `${item.id} (Buy)`, {
            customTitle: `${item.id} (Buy)`,
            description: [`Costs: ${item.cost} Gold Coins`],
            image: item.img,
            buyButtonText: ""Purchase"",
            canBuy: true
        });
    }

    // SELL SHOP (Buyable items)
    for (const item of buyItems) {
        api.createShopItemForPlayer(pId, ""Sell Shop"", `${item.id} (Sell)`, {
            customTitle: `${item.id} (Sell)`,
            description: [`Reward: ${sellPrices[item.id]} Gold Coins`],
            image: item.img,
            buyButtonText: ""Sell Item"",
            canBuy: true
        });
    }

    // SELL SHOP (Loot items)
    for (const id in sellOnlyItems) {
        api.createShopItemForPlayer(pId, ""Sell Shop"", `${id} (Sell)`, {
            customTitle: `${id} (Sell)`,
            description: [`Reward: ${sellOnlyItems[id]} Gold Coins`],
            image: id,
            buyButtonText: ""Sell Item"",
            canBuy: true
        });
    }
};

// =========================
// BUY + SELL LOGIC
// =========================

onPlayerBoughtShopItem = (pId, cat, itemKey, item) => {

    const coins = api.getInventoryItemAmount(pId, ""Gold Coin"");

    // BUY LOGIC
    for (const it of buyItems) {
        if (itemKey === `${it.id} (Buy)`) {
            if (coins >= it.cost) {
                api.removeItemName(pId, ""Gold Coin"", it.cost);
                api.giveItem(pId, it.id, 1);
                api.sendOverShopInfo(pId, `BOUGHT 1 ${it.id}`);
            } else {
                api.sendMessage(pId, `Need ${it.cost} Gold Coins`);
            }
            return;
        }
    }

    // SELL LOGIC (Buyable items)
    for (const it of buyItems) {
        if (itemKey === `${it.id} (Sell)`) {
            if (api.getInventoryItemAmount(pId, it.id) >= 1) {
                api.removeItemName(pId, it.id, 1);
                api.giveItem(pId, ""Gold Coin"", sellPrices[it.id]);
                api.sendOverShopInfo(pId, `SOLD 1 ${it.id}`);
            } else {
                api.sendMessage(pId, `No ${it.id} to sell`);
            }
            return;
        }
    }

    // SELL LOGIC (Loot items)
    for (const id in sellOnlyItems) {
        if (itemKey === `${id} (Sell)`) {
            if (api.getInventoryItemAmount(pId, id) >= 1) {
                api.removeItemName(pId, id, 1);
                api.giveItem(pId, ""Gold Coin"", sellOnlyItems[id]);
                api.sendOverShopInfo(pId, `SOLD 1 ${id}`);
            } else {
                api.sendMessage(pId, `No ${id} to sell`);
            }
            return;
        }
    }
};

// =========================
// WALLET DISPLAY
// =========================

tick = () => {
    const ids = api.getPlayerIds();
    for (const id of ids) {
        const amount = api.getInventoryItemAmount(id, ""Gold Coin"");
        api.setClientOption(id, ""RightInfoText"", `{Press B or Trolly icon to See shop} WALLET: ${amount} Gold Coins 🪙`);
    }
};

Take inspiration from the code so that when you want to make or edit new shop items, you can follow some of this code as inspiration and help!"
"btw, here is what the official GitHub docs say abt mob settings in  code! 
# Mob Settings

These impact the behaviour of mobs, what they look like, and how they sound. These can either be set on a per-mob basis or the default can be set for all mobs of a particular type.
These API methods allow you to modify mob settings:

```js
/**
 * Returns the current default value for a mob setting.
 *
 * @param {TMobType} mobType
 * @param {TMobSetting} setting
 * @returns {MobSettings<TMobType>[TMobSetting]}
 */
getDefaultMobSetting(mobType, setting)

/**
 * Set the default value for a mob setting.
 *
 * @param {TMobType} mobType
 * @param {TMobSetting} setting
 * @param {MobSettings<TMobType>[TMobSetting]} value
 * @returns {void}
 */
setDefaultMobSetting(mobType, setting, value)

/**
 * Get the current value of a mob setting for a specific mob.
 *
 * @param {MobId} mobId
 * @param {TMobSetting} setting
 * @param {boolean} [returnDefaultIfNotOverridden] - If true, return the default setting if not overridden.
 * @returns {MobSettings<MobType>[TMobSetting]}
 */
getMobSetting(mobId, setting, returnDefaultIfNotOverridden)

/**
 * Set the current value of a mob setting for a specific mob.
 *
 * @param {MobId} mobId
 * @param {TMobSetting} setting
 * @param {MobSettings<MobType>[TMobSetting]} value
 * @returns {void}
 */
setMobSetting(mobId, setting, value)
```

Here is the full list of available mob settings:


## attackDamage

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## attackEffectDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## attackEffectName

**Type:** `string`

**Example:** `null`

 



## attackImpulse

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## attackInterval

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## attackItemName

**Type:** `string`

**Example:** `null`

 



## attackRadius

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## attackSound

**Type:** `string`

**Example:** `null`

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## baseJumpImpulseY

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## baseRunningSpeed

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `4.55 * 0.85`

 



## baseWalkingSpeed

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `3.5`

 



## burstAttackInfo

**Type:** ` { burstAttackIntervals: readonly number[]; } `

**Example:** `null`

 



## chaseRadius

**Type:** `number`

**Example:** `0`

 



## combatTetherInfo

**Example:** ```ts
{
	range: 11,
	particleOpts: {
		texture: ""soul_0"",
		colorGradients: [
			{
				timeFraction: 0,
				minColor: [245, 35, 25, 1],
				maxColor: [255, 45, 35, 1],
			},
		],
	},
}
```

 



## evadeInfo

**Type:** `MobEvadeInfo`

**Example:** ```ts
{
	probability: 0.6,
	minAngle: Math.PI * 0.35,
	maxAngle: Math.PI * 0.6,
	impulse: 8,
}
```

Draugr Reaver: ""default""
NPC: ""default"", ""emma"", ""leo"", ""isabel"", ""sanjay"", ""imara"", ""enoch"", ""sara"", ""carmen""
Bobino Musculino: ""default""
Capitano Explovissimo: ""default""
```

## Mob AI

A mob's AI state determines its behaviour, e.g.: whether it is stood still, walking in a straight line, chasing someone, running towards a coordinate, etc. These API methods allow you to modify a mob's AI state:

```js
/**
 * Gets the current AI state for the given mob.
 *
 * @param {MobId} mobId
 * @returns { { state: MobAiState; params: MobAiStateParams<MobAiState> } }
 */
getMobAiState(mobId)

/**
 * Sets the current AI state for the given mob.
 * Some AI states will require context such as the ID of the lifeform being chased.
 *
 * @param {MobId} mobId
 * @param {TState} state
 * @param {MobAiStateParams<TState>} params
 * @returns {void}
 */
setMobAiState(mobId, state, params)
```

Here is the full list of available mob AI states and their parameters:

| State | Description | Parameters |
|-------|-------------|------------|
| `idle` | The mob is stood still, but it still has awareness of its environment.<br>For example: if the mob is hostile, it will still chase and attack nearby players. | `null` |
| `disabled` | The mob is stood still, and it has no awareness of its environment.<br>It will not even react if provoked. | `null` |
| `idleBeforeTurning` | The mob is stood still (idle) and is about to turn. | `null` |
| `turning` | The mob has chosen a new direction at random and is turning to face it. | `null` |
| `idleBeforeWalking` | The mob is stood still (idle) and is about to walk. | `null` |
| `walking` | The mob is walking in the direction it is facing. | `null` |
| `runningAway` | The mob is running away from the target lifeform. | `{ targetId: LifeformId }` |
| `chasing` | The mob is chasing the target lifeform. | `{ targetId: LifeformId }` |
| `following` | The mob is following the target lifeform.<br>It will stop if it is within the `minFollowingDistance` (mob setting) of the target,<br>and teleport to the target if it is outside the `maxFollowingDistance` (mob setting) of the target. | `{ targetId: LifeformId }` |
| `watching` | The mob is stood still looking at the target. | `{ targetId: LifeformId }` |
| `walkingToPosition` | The mob is walking towards the position.<br>It will stop if it is within the `stoppingRadius` (mob setting) of the position. | `{ pos: Pos }` |
| `runningToPosition` | The mob is running towards the position.<br>It will stop if it is within the `stoppingRadius` (mob setting) of the position. | `{ pos: Pos }` |"

"also press to code boards can trigger code even if isolated, however its recommended to just attach the press to code boards to a code block as press to code boards have a VERY limited character limit. The best use for press to code boards is for marking the function of a code block
For example:
Press to code
/* Code Block for jumping */

Ex2:
Press to Code
api.sendMessage(playerId, ""hi"");
(Note: sometimes the character limit can be so restrictive; it doesn't t change but its not rare if a single line of code cant fit on a press to code boards)"
"also, here is what the official bloxd wiki says abt Command aka Action boards:
Command Boards
Owner-Only Boards
If you are the owner of an Open World, you can create special Boards that activate when you [Right-Click] or press [E]. There are nine types of boards: 'press to get' Boards, 'press to lobby' Boards, 'press to tp' Boards, 'press to buy' Boards, 'press to mode' Boards, 'press to damage' Boards, 'press to execute' Boards, 'press to message' Boards 'press to sell' and 'press to equip' Boards.

If there are multiple interactive boards adjacent to each other (e.g. a board is directly above, below, to the left/right of, or behind another board), pressing any of those boards executes all the actions on the boards adjacent to each other. You can edit 'Press to __' Boards by Right Clicking/Pressing ""E"".

'Press to get' Board
“press to get” boards gives a certain number of items when they press the board. To create one, you need to type these on the Board. Note that “press to get” boards must be written in the small font size, and the “press to get” must be written in a separate line from the item name and number of items,

press to get
[Number of Items] [Item]
If you do not write the number for the number of items, it is taken as 1. If you do not have enough room on one line, such as '999 Moonstone Orb', you can put the 'Moonstone Orb' bit on a new line, and it would still work. The game would only accept the exact name of the items to successfully create a “press to get” board. (For example, you cannot create a “press to get” board by writing “diamond block” as the item, and only “Diamond Block” where both words are capitalised will be accepted)

You can also put the number of items as (smaller number)-(larger number), such that you will get a random number of items between the smaller number and larger number when you press the board. (e.g. putting “press to get 0-100 diamonds” will make you receive between 0 to 100 diamonds each time you press the board)

'Press to lobby' Board
A press to lobby Board redirects you to another Worlds lobby (but not lobbies of any other game modes). To create one, type these on the Board:

press to lobby
[Name of Lobby]
'Press to tp' Board
A press to tp Board teleports you to the specified coordinates. To create one, you need to type these on the Board:

press to tp
[X-Y-Z coordinates (e.g. 100 0 100)]
'Press to buy' Board
A press to buy Board allows players to buy a specific block or item for a certain price. The item sold and the item used as the cost can only be official items in the game. To create a “press to buy” board, you need to type the following on the Board. Note that you need to write “press to buy”, the item and the number of items you can buy, “costs”, and the price of the item to buy in four separate lines.

You can also put “nothing” or “everything” as the price of the item. (Self-explanatory)

press to buy
[number of items] [name of item]
costs
[number of Items to buy] [name of item (can also be “nothing” or “everything”)]
'Press to sell' Boards
A press to sell Board allows users to sell a certain item for a certain price. To create one, you need to type the following on the Board:

press to sell
[number of Items ] [name of item]
for
[number of Items to buy ] [name of item]
'Press to buy nothing costs everything' Boards
Added on December 10, 2024. This board is used to clear your inventory.

press to buy
nothing
costs
everything
'Press to damage' Boards
Damages a player by a specific amount of health when pressed, regardless of the armour the player is wearing. This number can also be a negative number to increase the player’s health.

press to damage
[Amount of health (eg. 5)]
'Press to mode' Boards
Added on December 10, 2024, this table is used to change your game mode. Currently, there are five game modes in world lobbies which are survival, peaceful, creative, survivaladventure, peaceful adventure and spectator. They are not capitalisation-sensitive and can be contracted as s, p, c, sa, pa and sp on the boards respectively.

press to mode
[mode (on Worlds)] (e.g. survival)]
'Press to execute' Boards
“press to execute” boards are used a run the action on a board or code block at another place.

press to execute
[X-Y-Z coordinates (e.g. 100 0 100) of the board or code block]
'Press to message' Boards
Added on December 10, 2024. This board is used to send a message to everyone in their chat, but only across your device’s screen as well.

press to message
[lobby message you want (e.g. Hi!)]
'Press to equip' boards
Added on August 28 2025. “Press to equip” boards will give and equip you armour when pressed.

Press to equip
[armour part (e.g. Diamond Helmet)]
Universal Boards
These Boards can be created in game modes other than creative, such as Greenville.

“Press to buy from chest” Boards
Similar to a press to buy Board, but it must be placed directly in front of a (adjacent to) Chest and the Chest must contain enough items to be sold to players. When you press on “press to buy from chest” boards and have enough items to buy the item sold, your items will be placed in the chest automatically and you will receive the item sold from the chest in your inventory.

If the chest runs out of the items to be sold, the message “cannot buy (item that can be bought from the chest. Short as “item”): chest does not contain enough (item)” will be shown in your chat when you press the board. If the chest runs out of space to put the items used to buy the item sold, pressing on the chest will cause the message “cannot buy (item): chest is full” to appear in your chat.

press to buy
from chest
[number of Items] [name of item]
costs
[number of items] [name of item]
'Press to sell to chest' Boards
Similar to a press to sell Board, but it must be placed on a Chest and the Chest must contain enough items that will be paid to players. This is substantially the same as “press to buy from chest” boards, except that you are using an item to trade for another item by “selling” instead of “buying”.

press to sell
to chest
[number of Items (e.g. 5)] [item (e.g. Maple Log)]
for
[number of Items to(e.g. 1)] [item to (e.g. Gold Bar)]
Trivia
If multiple boards are placed adjacent to each other, pressing on any of the board executes the actions on all the boards.
Boards were the first writable Item in the game.
People used to write press to buy Boards in the lobby after the leak of it resulting to people getting rich.
On August 4, 2024, there was a bug in Greenville where items could be obtained using the press to get Board, making it possible to obtain illegal items.
In a Greenville glitch, you can use Press to get Board to get items that are banned in Greenville (e.g. Fireball, Iceball, Moonstone Explosive), which are very useful as they can be used to break blocks that are almost impossible to find (e.g. Emerald Block, Lapis Block, Invisible Solid).
Previously, you cannot be able to edit a 'Press to __' board after making it, but now you can.
If you're muted, you cannot use boards to communicate with other players.
Before if a press to tp board and a press to get board were next to each other, you could spam the press to get board to get extra items, this has been fixed
Boards used to be able to convert the symbols -> and <- into symbols → and ← respectively, but have been patched due to new font settings.

Also, Boards are just signs (Minecraft signs without the pole/stick thing) and players would call it signs, you don't have to refer to it as boards ALL the time :)"

"here is what the official GitHub documentation says abt Client Options:
# Client Options

A player's ""Client Options"" impact what they are capable of doing in the world. For example you can give them a double jump by setting the player's `airJumpCount` to `1`. Alternatively their health bar can be increased by setting their `maxHealth` to `200`. These API methods allow you to modify the client options:

```js
/**
 * Modify a client option at runtime and send to the client if it changed
 *
 * @param {PlayerId} playerId
 * @param {PassedOption} option - The name of the option
 * @param {ClientOptions[PassedOption]} value - The new value of the option
 * @returns {void}
 */
setClientOption(playerId, option, value)

/**
 * Returns the current value of a client option
 *
 * @param {PlayerId} playerId
 * @param {PassedOption} option
 * @returns {ClientOptions[PassedOption]}
 */
getClientOption(playerId, option)

/**
 * Modify client options at runtime
 *
 * @param {PlayerId} playerId
 * @param {Partial<ClientOptions>} optionsObj - An object which contains key value pairs of new settings. E.g {canChange: true, speedMultiplier: false}
 * @returns {void}
 */
setClientOptions(playerId, optionsObj)

/**
 * Sets a client option to its default value. This will be the value stored in your game's defaultClientOptions, otherwise Bloxd's default.
 *
 * @param {PlayerId} playerId
 * @param {ClientOption} option
 * @returns {void}
 */
setClientOptionToDefault(playerId, option)
```

Here is the full list of available client options:


## airAccScale

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Amount of acceleration to apply to airborne players.

 Only change if absolutely necessary i.e. Rocket Obby uses 0.25.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## airFrictionScale

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Amount of friction to apply to airborne players.

 Only change if absolutely necessary i.e. Rocket Obby uses 0.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## airJumpCount

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Amount of air jumps the player has

 



## airMomentumConservation

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether to allow the player to strafe and conserve momentum while airborne.

 Only change if absolutely necessary i.e. only Rocket Obby uses true.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## arrowPotionEffectDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `6000`

 

Duration of arrow potion effects

 



## auraPerLevel

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `100`

 

How much Aura XP is required per level.

 



## autoRespawn

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

If true, player will respawn automatically after secsToRespawn seconds

 



## bounciness

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

How much the player bounces off of solid blocks.

 A value of 1 is equivalent to every block acting as a mushroom.

 



## bunnyhopMaxMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1.3`

 

Maximum multiplier for jump height when bunnyhopping

 



## cameraRoll

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Roll angle of the camera in radians. Useful for disorientation effects, death effects, etc.

 



## cameraRollTransitionMs

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Duration in ms to animate/transition to the camera roll angle (when you change cameraRoll). 0 = instant.

 Useful for smooth camera roll transitions.

 



## cameraTint

**Type:** `[number, number, number, number]`

**Default:** `null`

 

RGBA array [r, g, b, a] for camera screen tint effect. Values fall between 0 and 1.

 



## canAltAction

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can use the alt action key (right click on PC)

 



## canChange

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can change blocks

 



## canClimbWalls

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether the player can climb walls

 



## canCraft

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether to allow the player to craft items

 useFullInventory must be true for this to work

 



## canCrouch

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can crouch

 



## canCustomiseChar

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can customise their character

 



## canPickBlocks

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can pick blocks (middle mouse click on PC), ignored if creative is false

 



## canPickUpItems

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether to allow the player to pick up items

 



## canSeeNametagsThroughWalls

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can see name tags through walls

 



## cantBreakError

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `null`

 

Error message for when the player fails to break a block

 



## cantBuildError

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `null`

 

Error message for when the player fails to place a block

 



## cantChangeError

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `""You cannot modify this block""`

 

Error message for when the player fails to change a block

 



## canUseZoomKey

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can use the zoom key

 



## chatChannels

**Type:** ` { channelName: string; elementContent: string | CustomTextStyling; elementBgColor: string; }[] `

**Default:** `null`

 

Allows player to select a channel that is passed as argument to onPlayerChat. See engineGameplayTypes.ts for expected format

 



## compassTarget

**Type:** `string | number | number[]`

**Default:** `[0, 0, 0]`

 

The target the compass will point towards

 



## creative

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether the player is in creative mode

 



## crosshairText

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `""""`

 

Text to display by the crosshair

 



## crouchingSpeed

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `2`

 

Speed multiplier for the player when crouching.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## crouchMobDetectionRadiusMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `2`

 

Mult for the radius within which mobs can detect the player when crouching.

 If a player's mult is 2, then mobs will think they are twice as far away.

 



## dealingDamageDefaultMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Mult for when the player hits neither a leg or a head. Only applies to guns

 



## dealingDamageHeadMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1.75`

 

Damage multiplier for when the player hits a head. Only applies to guns

 



## dealingDamageLegMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Damage multiplier for when the player hits a leg. Only applies to guns

 



## dealingDamageMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Damage multiplier for all types of damage

 



## defaultBlock

**Type:** `string`

**Default:** `""Block of Gold""`

 

The default block the player can change blocks to, used if canChange is true but useInventory is false

 



## droppedItemScale

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Scale factor to use for dropped item meshes

 



## effectDamageDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `8000`

 

Duration of the +damage effect from plum

 



## effectDamageReductionDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `13000`

 

Duration of +damage reduction effect from pear

 



## effectHealthRegenDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `5000`

 

Duration of +health regen effect from cherry

 



## effectSpeedDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `8000`

 

Duration of +speed effect from cracked coconut

 



## fallDamage

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether to deal damage to the player when they fall

 



## flySpeedMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1.5`

 

Multiplier for the flying speed in creative mode

 



## fogChunkDistanceOverride

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `null`

 

Fog distance which overrides graphic settings. Uses graphic settings if null.

 



## fogColourOverride

**Type:** `string`

**Default:** `null`

 

RGB string for fog colour override. e.g. #ffffff

 



## forcedCameraDirection

**Type:** `[number, number, number]`

**Default:** `null`

 

Force the camera to look in a specific direction [x, y, z]. Set to null to allow free camera movement.

 



## forcedCameraDirectionTransitionMs

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Duration in ms to animate/transition to the forced camera direction (when you change forcedCameraDirection). 0 = instant.

 Useful for smooth camera movements.

 



## groundFrictionScale

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Amount of friction to apply to grounded players.

 Only change if absolutely necessary i.e. Rocket Obby uses 3.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## healthRegenAmount

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0.05`

 

Fraction of max health that regens each regen tick

 



## healthRegenInterval

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `4000`

 

How often health regen is ticked

 



## healthRegenStartAfter

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `5000`

 

How long after a player receives damage to start regen again

 



## heldLightColourOverride

**Type:** `string`

**Default:** `null`

 

Held item light colour override - hex colour string e.g. #ffffff. Applied regardless of any held item.

 



## hideCoordinates

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

When true, hides world and chunk coordinates regardless of the player's setting.

 



## horizontalKnockbackMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Multiplier for horizontal knockback when dealing damage

 



## initialHealth

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `100`

 

Health upon joining or respawning. Can be null for the player to not have health

 



## initialShield

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Shield upon joining or respawning

 



## inventoryItemsMoveable

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether the player can move items in their inventory, only applicable if useInventory is true

 



## invincible

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether the player is invincible

 



## jumpAmount

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `8`

 

Amount of jump power the player has

 



## killstreakDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `200000000`

 

Duration before a killstreak expires. (defaults to never expiring)

 



## lightingOverride

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `null`

 

When null, just use the player's graphics setting. When set, forces lighting on (true) or off (false).

 



## lobbyLeaderboardInfo

**Type:** `LobbyLeaderboardInfo`

**Default:** ```ts
{
	name: {
		displayName: ""Name"",
		sortPriority: 0,
	},
}
```

 

Columns of the lobby leaderboard

 



## maxAuraLevel

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

The maximum Aura Level attainable - Set to 0 to disable Aura XP

 



## maxHealth

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `100`

 

Maximum health the player can have

 



## maxPlayerZoom

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `15`

 

Maximum camera zoom level for the player

 



## maxShield

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `100`

 

Maximum shield the player can have

 



## middleTextLower

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `""""`

 

Small text to display in the middle of the screen

 



## middleTextUpper

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `""""`

 

Large text to display in the middle of the screen

 



## minChunkAddDist

**Type:** `[number, number]`

**Default:** `[2, 2]`

 

Minimum size of region around player where chunks are loaded.

 Format [horizontalMinChunkRadius, verticalMinChunkRadius].

 Each value should be between 2 and 4.

 

 We recommend leaving this at the default of [2, 2] unless you have a specific reason to change it.

 (e.g. you need players to see the bottom of a dropper)

 This is because higher values can be laggier on low-end devices.

 



## movementBasedFovScale

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Amount that player camera is affected by movement based fov

 



## music

**Type:** `Song`

**Default:** `null`

 

The music track to play in the background

 



## musicVolumeLevel

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0.6`

 

Volume level for the music

 



## numClosestPlayersVisible

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `null`

 

If set, clients will only be able to see the closest x players (good for client perf in games with many players)

 



## playerZoom

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Default camera zoom level for the player

 



## potionEffectDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `12000`

 

Duration of potion effects

 



## proximityFadeDistance

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0.625`

 

Distance in blocks over which we reduce the opacity of entities as they approach the camera.

 



## proximityFadeMinOpacity

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0.5`

 

Minimum opacity multiplier reachable when fading entities based on camera proximity.

 The player's own model is always able to fade to 0, and entities being ridden stay fully opaque (as if this value was 1).

 



## receivingDamageMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Damage multiplier for all types of incoming damage

 



## respawnButtonText

**Type:** `string`

**Default:** `""general:respawn""`

 

Text to show on respawn button. (E.g. ""Spectate"")

 



## RightInfoText

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `""""`

 

Text to display in the right info box

 



## runningSpeed

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `7`

 

Running speed for the player.

 STRONGLY recommend using `speedMultiplier` unless you have a specific use case for this, runningSpeed doesn't make UX sense on mobile.

 (Walking speed is ignored for mobile players, mobile player speed is determined by joystick input and the max of runningSpeed & walkingSpeed).

 

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 

 The only use case for walkingSpeed/runningSpeed over speedMultiplier or speed effects is to disable running or to inverse walking/running (so you run by default and e.g. hold shift to go slower).

 



## secsToRespawn

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `5`

 

After dying the player can respawn after this many seconds

 



## showBasicMovementControls

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether to show basic movement controls

 



## showKillfeed

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether to show the killfeed

 



## showPlayersInUnloadedChunks

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether to show the player in unloaded chunks

 



## showProgressBar

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

Whether to show the progress bar

 



## skyBox

**Type:** `string | EarthSkyBox`

**Default:** `""default""`

 

Not recommended to use anything other than ""default"" as client FPS can drop while loading the skybox

 



## skyLightColourOverride

**Type:** `string`

**Default:** `null`

 

Sky light colour override - hex string e.g. #ffffff.

 



## speedMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Speed multiplier for the player.

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 



## splashPotionEffectDuration

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `8000`

 

Duration of splash potion effects

 



## stompDamageMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Mult for the damage done by ""stomping"" on a lifeform, i.e.: falling on them wearing Spiked Boots.

 



## stompDamageRadius

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `0`

 

Radius around the player that will be affected by the stomp damage.

 



## strictFluidBuckets

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether a player can place fluid when canChange is false

 



## touchscreenActionButton

**Type:** `string | CustomTextStyling`

**Default:** `null`

 

The contents of the action button. Supports custom text styling. onTouchscreenActionButton will be called when button pressed.

 



## ttbMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Multiplier for the time to break any block

 



## useFullInventory

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

For now just enables the UI of the full inventory

 



## useInventory

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `true`

 

Whether to allow the player to use the inventory

 Disabling this will also disable the hotbar

 



## usePlayAgainButton

**Type:** `boolean`

**Default:** `false`

 

When player is dead, also show a play again button to matchmake player into a new lobby. Mostly useful for sessionBased games

 



## verticalKnockbackMultiplier

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `1`

 

Multiplier for vertical knockback when dealing damage

 



## walkingSpeed

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `4`

 

Walking speed for the player.

 STRONGLY recommend using `speedMultiplier` unless you have a specific use case for this, walkingSpeed doesn't make UX sense on mobile.

 (Walking speed ignored for mobile players, mobile player speed is determined by joystick input and the max of runningSpeed & walkingSpeed).

 

 Players are used to the default bloxd movement behaviour and speed,

 and may be put off from your game if different muscle memory is required.

 We suggest applying speed or slowness effects instead, using api.applyEffect.

 

 The only use case for walkingSpeed/runningSpeed over speedMultiplier or speed effects is to disable running or to inverse walking/running (so you run by default and e.g. hold shift to go slower).

 



## zoomOutDistance

**Type:** `number`

**Default:** `3`

 

Distance to zoom the camera out to

 


(also btw when it says a value of 1 means every block acts like a mushroom, its referring to the Fat Mushroom block, which is incredibly bouncy. u can't edit the bounciness of this one specific thing)"
"official updated list of skins and poses for bloxd API coding:
# Skins and Poses

This document lists all API-selectable skins and poses that can be used with the game API.

## Usage

### Changing Player Skin

Use `api.changePlayerIntoSkin(playerId, part, skinName)` to change a player's skin:

```js
api.changePlayerIntoSkin(playerId, ""body"", ""body_0_0"")
api.changePlayerIntoSkin(playerId, ""head"", ""wizard"") // NPC skin
```

### Setting Player Pose

Use `api.setPlayerPose(playerId, pose)` to change a player's pose:

```js
api.setPlayerPose(playerId, ""standing"")
api.setPlayerPose(playerId, ""sitting"")
```

---

## Poses (7 poses)

```
standing
sitting
zombie
gliding
driving
sleeping
riding
```

---

## Skin Parts

The following skin parts can be customised: `skin`, `hat`, `head`, `eyebrows`, `eyes`, `back`, `body`, `legs`, `shoes`

---

## NPC Skins (12 skins)

These are special full-body skins typically used for NPCs. Apply them via the `head` part:

```js
api.changePlayerIntoSkin(playerId, ""head"", ""wizard"")
```

```

monster_hunter_lorenzo

```

---

## Notes

- Skin names ending with `_none` are internal variants and cannot be used via the API.
- Skin variants follow the pattern `{part}_{style}_{colour}` (e.g., `body_0_0`, `head_1_2`).
- NPC skins apply a complete character appearance and override individual part selections."
"updtaed official list on used able sounda dn music while coding: # Sounds and Songs

This document lists all available sound effects and songs that can be used in game modes.

---

## Sound Effects

Sound effects are short audio clips (e.g., footsteps, hits, ambient sounds). Use the `playSound`, or `broadcastSound` API methods.

### API Usage

```ts
// Play a sound for a specific player
api.playSound(playerId, soundName, volume, rate, posSettings?)

// Broadcast a sound to all players (or all except one)
api.broadcastSound(soundName, volume, rate, posSettings?, exceptPlayerId?)
```

**Parameters:**
- `soundName`: One of the sound names listed below
- `volume`: 0.0 to 1.0
- `rate`: Playback rate (1.0 = normal speed, 0.5 = half speed, 2.0 = double speed)
- `posSettings` (optional): `{ playerIdOrPos: PlayerId | number[], maxHearDist?: number, refDistance?: number }`

**Tip:** If you want a random similar sound, remove the number suffix from the sound name (e.g., use `""grass""` instead of `""grass1""`).

## Music

Music tracks are longer audio for background ambiance. Use the `setClientOption` API method with the `""music""` option.

### API Usage

```ts
// Play a song for a specific player
api.setClientOption(playerId, ""music"", ""Adigold - Dreamless Sleep"")

// Stop the current song
api.setClientOption(playerId, ""music"", null)
```

**Note:** Unlike sound effects, songs are played via client options, not the `playSound` API. Only one song plays at a time per player; setting a new song replaces the current one.
